Separately, start the `[data-scroll="middle"]`/`"end"` left ramp at 3rem in src/pages/planlegger/index.astro:321-336 so it clears the hour rail's 21-56px labels.
- Verifier: Attacked it by re-measuring at 360x740 to see whether the hint is ever honest: at 360 the fre column's right edge (352 in scroll coords, 376 unscrolled) does exceed the frame's right edge (344) by 8px, so the hint is defensible there — but at the assigned 390 width every day header is inside the frame and the hint is simply false. Also checked whether the 26px overflow could be real content rather than padding: computed `padding: 24px/24px` and grid width 336 = grid min-width, so 384 - 358 = 26 is padding only. Also confirmed the mask/rail overlap by reading the screenshot back rather than trusting the geometry. Kept at minor (false copy + a legibility hit, not a blocked flow).
### [mob-7] .np-field renders a 44px-tall control whose top and bottom 11px do nothing on tap — only the middle 21px focuses the input
- Where: `src/styles/primitives.css:250` · a11y · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: Reproduced with real taps at both 360x740 and 390x844 on `/emner/`. `.np-field.emner-search` box = x16 y181.03 w358 h44 (its `min-height: var(--control-h)` at primitives.css:237 correctly resolves to 44px on a coarse pointer), while `#emner-search` inside it = x28 y192.61 w334 h20.83 — `align-items: center` (primitives.css:235) centres it and `.np-field input` (primitives.css:250-257) never stretches. `mouse.click(x + w/2, y + 3)` -> `document.activeElement` = `BODY`; `mouse.click(x + w/2, y + h/2)` -> `INPUT#emner-search`. Identical at 360. The dead bands are 11.6px at the top and 11.6px at the bottom of a control that looks 44px tall, and the field is a `<div>` with no wrapping `<label>`, so nothing else picks the tap up.
- Failure: A student taps the upper edge of the large search box on /emner/ — the page's only control on an otherwise empty screen — and the keyboard does not appear, with nothing to explain why. The same geometry applies to every `.np-field` on the site, including the programme typeahead that is step one of the §0 flow.
- Fix: In src/styles/primitives.css:250-257 add `align-self: stretch;` to `.np-field input` (add `line-height: 1` if the baseline drifts) so the input fills the field's full 44px and the whole visible box is the hit target.
- Verifier: Attacked it by looking for a `<label for>` wrapper or a JS click handler on the field that would forward focus — the field is a plain `<div class="np-field">` and the real tap test returns BODY, so there is none. Also checked whether the miss only happens at an unrealistic 3px offset: the input's top edge is 11.6px below the field's, so any tap in the top or bottom quarter of the visible control is dead. Did not independently re-verify the studieinfo-modal and add-course-dialog instances the auditor cites, but they use the same `.np-field` rule so the geometry follows. Kept at minor — the centre of the control (where the placeholder text is) works, so most taps land.
### [mob-8] The catalog result list and the exam list keep the UA's 40px list indent, so rows are visibly misaligned from the search field above them and lose 12% of a phone's width
- Where: `src/pages/emner/index.astro:48` · design-system · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: Reproduced at 360x740 with a real query. `/emner/` searching "matematikk" (112 hits): `<ul class="emner-results">` computed `padding-inline-start: 40px`, ul box x 16 w 328, but every `<li>` renders at x 56 w 288. Screenshot v-emner-360.png shows the consequence plainly: the search field and the Gjøvik/Levanger/Trondheim/Ålesund chips start at x 16, the "112 treff" counter starts at x 16, and then the result rows step 40px right into an empty gutter with no bullet (the rule sets `list-style: none` at emner/index.astro:49) — and "Matematikk for Informatikk" wraps onto two lines inside the narrowed 288px. Identical on `/planlegger/`'s exam ribbon: `<ul class="exam-list">` computed `padding-inline-start: 40px`, ul x 16 w 328, rows x 56 w 288 ("to 26. nov TMA4412 om 122 dager", "5 dager mellomrom", "ti 1. des TMA4400"). base.css:59-68's `:where(p, ul, ol):not([class])` correctly scopes the prose measure away from classed lists (REVIEW D6) but nothing resets the UA padding, and neither emner/index.astro:48-54 nor planlegger/index.astro:379-382 zeroes it.
- Failure: A student searches "matematikk" on a 360px phone. The result rows sit 40px right of everything else on the page including the search box that produced them, reading as a misaligned layout, and course names that would fit on one line wrap onto two — on the page whose only job is scanning 112 results.
- Fix: Add `padding-inline-start: 0` to `.emner-results` (src/pages/emner/index.astro:48) and to `:global(.exam-list)` (src/pages/planlegger/index.astro:379). Better, put `:where(ul, ol)[class] { padding-inline-start: 0 }` beside the existing measure rule in src/styles/base.css:66 so the other classed data lists (`.planner-notes-list`, `.details-reductions-list`, `.details-activities-list`) inherit the same decision.
- Verifier: Attacked the auditor's version, which measured an EMPTY list (height 0) and inferred the row inset. I re-ran with an actual search and confirmed real `<li>` boxes at x 56 w 288, and separately confirmed `.exam-list` with three real exam rows on a loaded plan. Also checked whether some rule elsewhere resets it — grepped `emner/index.astro` and `planlegger/index.astro`, both set only `list-style: none` and `max-width: none`. The severity claim ("halving how many hits fit") is overstated — the rows are 104-129px tall for other reasons — so I re-anchored the evidence on the visible misalignment instead. Survived at minor.
### [modals-4] A dropped programme course shows "Lagt til ✓" in the add modal and its add control is inert
- Where: `src/components/planner/addCourse.ts:210` · correctness · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: addCourse.ts:210 drives row state from `deps.store.hasCourse(code)`; store.ts:325-327 is `loadPlan().courses.some(c => c.code === code)` — it never consults `dropped`, unlike `activeCourses` (store.ts:207-209) which the grid, credit line and clash engine all use. Live on `#26h;MTDT.2026;`: clicked "Dropp" on TDT4109 in the rail (hash → `#26h;MTDT.2026;HMS0002,-TDT4109,TMA4400,TMA4412,EXPH0300`; rail row reads "TDT4109 | Legg tilbake | droppet — fortsatt en del av programmet"). Opened the add modal and searched TDT4109 → row textContent "TDT4109Informasjonsteknologi, grunnkursLegg tilLagt til ✓Fjern" with `addBtn.hidden === true` (i.e. the added state), and clicking the still-visible "Legg til" (visible only because of modals-1) left the hash unchanged at `…,-TDT4109,…` because `addCourse` no-ops on a code already present (store.ts:303-305).
- Failure: Student drops TDT4109, then searches it in "Legg til emne": the modal asserts "Lagt til ✓" for a course that is excluded from the grid, the credit total and the exam list, and the only control that responds does nothing.
- Fix: In addCourse.ts's `sync`, compute in-plan state from the active set (`activeCourses(deps.store.loadPlan()).some(c => c.code === code)`), and have the add button call `deps.store.restoreCourse(code)` when the code exists but is dropped.
- Verifier: Reproduced. Downgraded from major: the auditor's "there is no path back to the course" is wrong — the rail row for the dropped course is still on screen with a working "Legg tilbake" (verified in the same run), which is the surface D3 assigns the restore verb to. What survives is a false state label in a secondary surface plus a dead control; survivable, so minor.
### [modals-5] Studieinfo's Lagre commits the semester before it validates, so a rejected Lagre followed by Avbryt leaves the semester switched
- Where: `src/components/planner/studieinfo.ts:581` · correctness · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `commit()` calls `deps.store.setSemester(semesterId)` at studieinfo.ts:581, *before* the `program && cohort === null` guard at :585-589 returns early without closing the dialog. Reproduced live from a cleared localStorage: `np:lastSemester = null`, staged programme BALIT, selected "Vår 2027", pressed Lagre → hint "Velg kull for å lagre studieprogrammet.", dialog still open, and `np:lastSemester` already `"27v"` with the hash rewritten to `#27v;-;`. Pressing Avbryt → `{last:"27v", profile:null, hash:"#27v;-;"}`: the programme half was discarded as promised, the semester half was kept.
- Failure: Student on Høst 2026 opens studieinfo, switches the select to Vår 2027 to look ahead, picks a programme, forgets the kull, presses Lagre, is told to pick a kull, gives up and presses Avbryt. The dialog closes and the planner is now on Vår 2027 — a different semester with a different (and timetable-unpublished) course list — even though the only feedback they got was a message about what could *not* be saved.
- Fix: In studieinfo.ts `commit()`, move `deps.store.setSemester(semesterId)` below the `program && cohort === null` guard (still above `setProgramPlan`, which is why it runs first), so a rejected Lagre writes nothing at all.
- Verifier: Reproduced exactly. Downgraded from the auditor's major: (a) the ordering is a deliberate, commented decision (studieinfo.ts:577-580 — "Semester commits *unconditionally* and first, so a semester-only edit is never dropped by an incomplete programme half"), so the fix must preserve semester-only saves; (b) the switch is not "silent" — the planner behind the modal re-renders and the hash becomes `#27v;…`, both visible on close; (c) the user did press Lagre, and the rejection message is scoped to "studieprogrammet". The genuine defect that survives is the partial commit on a *rejected* Lagre, which the module header (studieinfo.ts:7-9) contradicts. Minor.
### [modals-6] The pre-add preview drops the off-semester warning exactly when the plan is empty (first add of a new visitor)
- Where: `src/components/site/planClash.ts:106` · ux · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: addCourse.ts:148-163 composes the row note from `clashSentence(planClash(...))`. planClash.ts:105-106 returns `{kind:"empty"}` before the candidate's own timetable is ever fetched when `activeCourses(plan).filter(c => c.code !== course.code).length === 0`, so the `off-semester` branch (planClash.ts:111) is unreachable for an empty plan. TDT4100 is a live instance: `/api/course/TDT4100/timetable?year=2026` returns 11 entries, all `term: "2026_VÅR"`, weeks `2-13`/`15-18`, versus 26h teaching weeks 34–47 in data/semesters.json. Verified both sides live on the same row: empty plan → "7,5 sp · Ingen andre emner i planen din for Høst 2026."; plan holding one course (`#26h;-;TMA4400`) → "7,5 sp · Undervises ikke i Høst 2026 — ingen kollisjon å sjekke."
- Failure: A first-time visitor with an empty plan searches TDT4100, reads "7,5 sp · Ingen andre emner i planen din for Høst 2026", adds it, and only then sees the rail say "undervises ikke i valgt semester" with the credit line at "0 av 30 sp" — the one warning that mattered was withheld precisely because they had nothing else in the plan yet.
- Fix: In planClash.ts, resolve the candidate's own `semesterLectures` before the `others.length === 0` early return so an empty plan can still yield `{kind:"off-semester"}` (falling through to `{kind:"empty"}` otherwise). Cheaper alternative in addCourse.ts's `attachPreview`: the bundle is already fetched there for credits, so check `entriesInSemester(bundle.timetable, deps.semester.teachingWeeks)` and prefer the off-semester sentence over the empty one.
- Verifier: Reproduced both branches on the same row. Downgraded from major and re-categorised: the auditor's DR-10 claim is a misread — DR-10 (PRODUCT.md:390) says an off-semester add "yields a `notices[]` line and is excluded from the credit total", which is post-add behaviour and does fire (rail note + "0 av 30 sp" + "1 emne undervises ikke i Høst 2026"). The `{kind:"empty"}` sentence is also literally true, not a false statement. What survives is a genuine inconsistency in the preview engine — the same course, same semester, gets warned about only when the plan is non-empty — hitting exactly the persona-B first add. Minor.
### [modals-7] Escape is wrong in both modals: swallowed by the add-course `type="search"` input, and closes the whole studieinfo dialog from inside the open typeahead
- Where: `src/components/planner/studieinfo.ts:320` · a11y · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: studieinfo.ts:319-322 does `event.stopPropagation(); // close the list, not the dialog`, but a dialog's Escape close is a CloseWatcher close *request*, which only `preventDefault()` cancels. Live: with the programme listbox open on "datateknologi", one Escape gave `{dialogOpen:false, listHidden:true}` — the whole modal went, taking the staged programme/semester edits with it. In the other direction addCourse.ts:84 sets `searchInput.type = "search"`; Chrome's search input consumes Escape to clear itself and cancels the close request, so with text in the field press 1 → `{open:true, value:""}` and press 2 → `{open:false}`. Separately, addCourse.ts:9-11's header claims "Esc/backdrop-adjacent dismissal is the native `showModal()` behaviour — nothing extra to wire"; clicking the backdrop at (20,20) left `dialog.open === true` and the dialog has no `closedby` attribute.
- Failure: Studieinfo: a student changes the semester, starts typing a programme name, presses Escape to dismiss the suggestion list (the combobox convention the code is explicitly aiming at) and instead loses the entire modal and every staged edit. Add modal: a keyboard user presses Escape to leave the dialog and the field silently empties instead, so the dismissal gesture reads as broken until they press it again.
- Fix: studieinfo.ts:319-322 — replace `event.stopPropagation()` with `event.preventDefault()` so the close request is actually cancelled while the listbox is open. addCourse.ts:84 — set `searchInput.type = "text"` (the native clear button is not load-bearing) and correct the header comment, since a native modal `<dialog>` has never dismissed on backdrop click.
- Verifier: Reproduced both halves in Chromium, including the two-press count and the studieinfo dialog closing from an open listbox. I dropped the auditor's implicit "backdrop dismissal is broken" claim to a comment-accuracy note: no browser closes `showModal()` dialogs on backdrop click without `closedby="any"`, so there is no regression there, only a wrong code comment. Kept at minor — both are recoverable in one extra keystroke/reopen.
### [pc-1] The browsable course-page grade chart that D12/§12 killed by name is back on /emne/[code]/, and PRODUCT.md still says it was removed
- Where: `src/pages/emne/[code].astro:131` · product-conformance · CONFIRMED · regression of U12
- Evidence: Verified in code and live. `src/pages/emne/[code].astro:131-137` declares `<section id="grades-section">` with the "Karakterer" heading and line 438 calls `void mountGradeChart(code, signal)`; `src/components/site/gradeChart.ts:34` renders `MAX_SEMESTERS = 6` bar charts. Live on /emne/TDT4100/: `.grades-chart` = 6, `.grades-bar` = 36. Against the docs: PRODUCT.md:252 states "The browsable grade chart was removed from /emne/[code]/ per D12 (shipped); nothing has replaced it in a decision cell yet"; PRODUCT.md:279 "grade stats are **not** a browsable course-page decoration"; D12 (PRODUCT.md:419) and §12's KILLED list both name it; docs/REVIEW.md:675 lists "Restoring the grade chart in any browsable form" under "Deliberately not recommended", and REVIEW.md:496 (U12) killed the previous incarnation. Commit 94b5d9a restored it; its design spec (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-27-course-page-and-week-density-design.md) never mentions D12, U12 or §12 — grep for "D12" in that file returns nothing — so the killed decision was neither honoured nor overturned, and no PRODUCT.md/ROADMAP line was updated. Phase 4's decision cell does not exist, so this is browsable, not in-decision.
- Failure: Not a user-facing malfunction — a doc/code contradiction on a named killed feature. The concrete failure is the next session's: it reads PRODUCT.md §6/§12/D12 plus REVIEW.md §10, concludes the chart is forbidden, and deletes 300 lines of deliberate, tested work; or the reverse, someone re-derives D12's parasitism argument and re-litigates it from scratch. Meanwhile the definitive product doc states as shipped fact something the site contradicts.
- Fix: Reconcile, don't reflexively delete. If the figure is intended (it was shipped deliberately one commit ago, with a design spec, e2e coverage and a real colour-validator argument), record a D16 in PRODUCT.md §10 superseding D12, correct PRODUCT.md:252 and :279 and REVIEW.md's §10 row, and note it in ROADMAP's shipped list. If D12 still stands, remove the `#grades-section` block (emne/[code].astro:131-137), the `mountGradeChart` call (line 438) and the `:global(.grades-*)` CSS, and park `lib/planner/grades.ts` for the Phase-4 decision cell. Either way pc-2 must be fixed first if it stays.
- Verifier: I tried hard to kill this as "a deliberate documented decision", which is the brief's own refutation ground. It fails: the only document authorising it (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-27-…) is not in the brief's authority order and does not adjudicate D12 at all, while PRODUCT.md — authority #2 — still asserts the chart is gone. I also refuted the auditor's *evidence*: I measured the page (Playwright bounding boxes, 1280×900) and the grades section is 458 px of a 1994 px page, SMALLER than the timetable section's 559 px, and the primary CTA "Legg til i planen" sits 850 px above it, not 900+. So "dominates the page / more than the timetable, exam block and CTA combined" is false and I dropped it. What survives is only the conformance contradiction, so I downgraded major → minor: nothing is broken for a user, the figure is well-built, masked-cell-aware and accessible.
### [pc-4] The DR-8 provenance line is stuck at "Henter timeplan fra NTNU nå" on every normal render — the steady-state branch is unreachable without an interaction
- Where: `src/components/planner/plannerApp.ts:1486` · correctness · CONFIRMED · regression of U9
- Evidence: Reproduced and then proved the mechanism. `renderProvenance()` has three call sites only (plannerApp.ts:1460 in `renderAll`, :1506 in `renderPlanDependents`, :1668 in the index-load `.then`); `loadBundles()`'s completion block (lines 1483-1486) calls `renderCreditLine / renderCourseRows / renderGapLine / renderGridAndExams` and not `renderProvenance`, so the line keeps whatever it composed while `states.some(s => s.loading)` was still true (line 1393). Live poll of `/planlegger/#26h;MTDT.2026;HMS0002,TDT4109,TMA4400,TMA4412,EXPH0300` every 2,5 s for 17,5 s: `#planner-grid-status` = "ingen kollisjoner" and 7 blocks drawn from t=2,5 s, while `#planner-provenance` read "Henter timeplan fra NTNU nå · eksamensdatoer fra katalogen (hentet 24. jul 2026) · studieplan for kull 2026. Uoffisiell." at every sample. Toggling "Vis øvinger og labber" does not fix it either (that handler routes to `renderGridAndExams` only). Clicking "Dropp" on a course — which routes through `renderAll` — flips it immediately to "Timeplan hentet direkte fra NTNU nå …", which proves it is a missing re-render and not a stuck `loading` flag.
- Failure: Every student who opens their week and does not edit it reads a fully rendered grid, a green verdict and a credit line, under a footer sentence claiming the timetable is still being fetched. The intended honest sentence ("Timeplan hentet direkte fra NTNU nå") is never seen on the normal path. PRODUCT.md §6 lists this line as the honest-join MUST, "composed per-render from what actually happened".
- Fix: Add `renderProvenance();` after `renderGridAndExams();` in `loadBundles()`'s completion block (src/components/planner/plannerApp.ts:1486). `retryBundles` (line 1197) goes through `loadBundles`, so it is covered by the same line. Worth an e2e assertion in e2e/flows.pw.ts that the provenance line does not contain "Henter" once the first `.planner-block` is visible.
- Verifier: I attacked the diagnosis rather than the symptom: the symptom could equally have been a `loading` flag never cleared (a much bigger bug) or an index-load ordering race that self-corrects. I ruled both out by forcing a `renderAll` via a "Dropp" click and watching the line correct itself in the same session, and by confirming the øvinger toggle (which does not call `renderAll`) leaves it stale. I could not find any other path that repaints it on a passive visit. Downgraded major → minor: the copy is wrong on every load, but it errs toward under-claiming ("still fetching") rather than asserting something false about the student's plan, and the fix is one line.
### [pc-7] PRODUCT.md and ROADMAP still describe the deleted sitewide plan count-link as shipped, and still call the shipped homepage proof fragment "the one MUST still open"
- Where: `docs/PRODUCT.md:202` · product-conformance · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: Each sub-claim checked individually. (1) PRODUCT.md:202 ("a count-link ('N emner · X sp → ukeplanen') shown on every page but the two that already show the plan"), :147 and :258 all describe the count-link as the shipped cross-page affordance; ROADMAP.md:38 lists "a studieinfo chip + a plan-count link, identical on every page" as shipped. Commit 665513f ("Remove the sitewide plan count-link bar") deleted it; `grep -rn "count-link" src/` returns nothing and the live DOM on /emner/ and /emne/TDT4100/ carries only the chip. (2) src/layouts/Layout.astro:14-22's own frontmatter doc comment still describes "a count-link back to the week (non-empty plans only, on every page but the two that already show the plan)" — stale in the file that used to own it. (3) The converse: PRODUCT.md:231 says the `.np-frame.np-ruled` proof fragment is "**not yet built** … the one MUST in this section still open" and ROADMAP.md's Phase 3 repeats it, but src/pages/index.astro:27-45 renders exactly that fragment — red-ink TDT4110/TMA4100 collision plus the "Del planen med en lenke — ingen innlogging" caption — verified live on /. (4) src/pages/404.astro:50 prints `Data hentet ${crawlDate} fra NTNU` and Layout.astro:170 prints the identical string in the footer of the same short page.
- Failure: A future session reads §4/ROADMAP, sees the count-link described as shipped connective tissue, and either re-adds the bar that was deliberately removed one commit ago or burns a pass "fixing" its absence; the same session reads §5/Phase 3 and rebuilds a homepage proof fragment that is already on the page. On /404 the visitor reads the same crawl-date sentence twice within 200 px.
- Fix: docs/PRODUCT.md: correct :147, :202 and :258 so the studieinfo chip alone carries the cross-page affordance (count-link removed 2026-07-27, commit 665513f), and mark :231's proof fragment shipped. docs/ROADMAP.md: drop "a plan-count link" from line 38 and the proof-fragment bullet from Phase 3. Fix the stale Layout.astro:14-22 header comment. Delete the duplicate line at src/pages/404.astro:50 (the footer already says it).
- Verifier: I tried to refute this as "docs drift is not a finding" and as "the brief says Phase 3 is deliberately not started, so the proof-fragment line is correct". Both fail: the brief puts PRODUCT.md at the top of the authority order for judging the shipped site, so a definitive doc asserting the opposite of the code is exactly this dimension's subject matter; and the Phase-3 defence is backwards here — the docs claim something is unbuilt that I verified rendering live on /. I checked every one of the four sub-claims against both the file and the running server rather than taking the auditor's word; all four hold. Kept at minor — no user is harmed except by the duplicated 404 sentence.
### [pd-4] No timeout or AbortSignal on any planner fetch, and `loadBundles` gates every re-render on `Promise.all` — one stalled course withholds the whole page
- Where: `src/lib/planner/data.ts:56` · correctness · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `fetchJson` is a bare `await fetch(url)` with no `signal` and no timeout (data.ts:56-69); `fetchCourseBundle` accepts no `AbortSignal` (data.ts:85-89); `mountPlannerApp` receives a `signal` and threads it to studieinfo/popover/listeners but never to a fetch. `loadBundles` awaits `Promise.all` over every course (plannerApp.ts:1473-1481) before its four re-renders. Live probe routing `**/api/course/TMA4400/timetable**` to a handler that never settles, plan of 3 courses: at t≈4 s, 16 s and 28 s the page is byte-identical — `#planner-grid-status` = "henter timeplan …", `#planner-credit-line` = "henter …", `#planner-exam-status` = "henter eksamensdatoer …", `.planner-block` count 0 (skeleton only). The two courses whose bundles resolved in under a second are never drawn. For contrast the worker→upstream hop is bounded (`AbortSignal.timeout` in ntnu-api's HttpClient); the browser→worker hop is not.
- Failure: One `/api/course/*/timetable` response stalls (mobile network handoff, captive portal, edge hiccup). The planner shows a skeleton and three "henter …" spinners with no error and no retry until Chrome's own socket timeout minutes later, even though the rest of the plan loaded instantly. The common, milder form: time-to-first-week is always set by the single slowest course.
- Fix: (1) data.ts:56/85 — accept an optional `signal` and pass `AbortSignal.any([signal, AbortSignal.timeout(15000)])` to `fetch`, so a stall becomes an ordinary captured entry in `bundle.errors`, which the partial-failure path already renders. (2) plannerApp.ts:1473-1486 — move the four re-renders into the per-course `.then` (microtask-debounced) so a slow course delays only its own row.
- Verifier: Reproduced the frozen page live for 28 s. Downgraded from the auditor's major: a true black-hole stall is rare (browsers do eventually abort), the state is degraded rather than wrong, and the everyday impact is a slower first paint rather than a broken one. Kept because the missing client-side timeout is a one-line-scope gap that also makes pd-5's failure path never fire for stalls.
### [pd-8] `?year=` is a no-op upstream, so a semester switch re-issues 2N requests — including the year-less details URL — for byte-identical data the grid may never draw
- Where: `src/lib/planner/data.ts:90` · perf · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `curl '/api/course/TDT4109/timetable?year=Y&version=1' | md5sum` gives the identical digest `2f19721806cc54a4b318a7f266cd9fef` for Y ∈ {2023, 2025, 2026, 2027}; the worker's cache key includes the year (worker/src/routes.ts:173) so this is genuinely upstream ignoring it. Live request log for `#26h;-;+TDT4109,+TMA4400,+TMA4412`: initial load = 6 `/api` requests; switching to `#27v;…` = 6 more, incl. `/api/course/TDT4109/timetable?year=2027&version=1` (identical bytes) **and** a re-issue of the year-less, version-less `/api/course/TDT4109` details URL, because `fetchCourseBundle`'s memo key is the composite `${code}:${year}:${version}` (data.ts:90) even though the details leg carries neither. Switching back = 0 requests (memo hit). Worse: of the three semesters `candidateSemesters` offers today (26h, 27v, 27h), only 26h has `timetablePublished: true` in data/semesters.json — so both cross-year switches fetch every timetable and then render the "publiseres vanligvis i …" fallback card instead of a grid, discarding the data unshown. The comment at plannerApp.ts:842-843 states an invariant ("A bundle fetched for 26h is 2026 data") the upstream does not honour.
- Failure: Student toggles Høst 2026 → Vår 2027 to compare terms. Every course's timetable *and* details are refetched against NTNU, the week drops to a skeleton for the round trip (210-365 ms per request warm, more cold), and the result is thrown away behind an unpublished-semester fallback card. CLAUDE.md's crawler-politeness posture is undercut by the client re-asking for data it holds.
- Fix: Smallest safe change: memoise the details leg under its own `code` key rather than the composite `code:year:version` (data.ts:90-104) — the URL has no year, so this is provably a duplicate request. Second: skip `loadBundles` entirely when `currentSemester().timetablePublished === false` (plannerApp.ts:1466), since the grid renders the fallback card regardless. Third, at minimum, correct the comment at plannerApp.ts:842-843 so a future reader does not trust a year-scoping guarantee the API does not give.
- Verifier: Verified the year no-op byte-for-byte across four years and checked it is not the worker's cache flattening them (the cache key carries the year). Measured the real fan-out myself rather than trusting the auditor's "26 more requests" (that number included programme-plan calls; the honest figure is 2N per new year, 0 on returning). Killed the auditor's proposed fix of dropping the semester bundle reset — that would be unsafe if upstream ever honours year — and replaced it with the two changes that are safe today. Severity minor: waste and a false comment, no wrong output.
### [pd-9] Raw upstream English error strings render inside the Norwegian UI ("fikk ikke hentet detaljer: Not found")
- Where: `src/lib/planner/data.ts:61` · copy · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `fetchJson` uses the response body's `error` field verbatim as the Error message (data.ts:61-66); `fetchCourseBundle` prefixes it with a Norwegian label (lines 110/117) and `renderCourseRows` prints `fikk ikke hentet ${error}` (plannerApp.ts:954). The worker emits English: `curl /api/course/ZZZ9999` → 404 `{"error":"Not found"}`; `errorJson("Invalid course code", 400)`, `errorJson("Rate limited", 429)` (worker/src/routes.ts:87/90/108), and `mapUpstreamError` passes ntnu-api's `err.message` through unchanged on 502. That the body's string is rendered verbatim is proven live: with an injected `{"error":"Upstream boom"}` the row read exactly "fikk ikke hentet timeplan: Upstream boom". A transport failure is worse — `fetch` rejects with a TypeError whose message is the browser's "Failed to fetch".
- Failure: A student opens a shared plan link containing a decommissioned or mistyped course code (or hits a 502/429). The one line explaining what went wrong reads "fikk ikke hentet detaljer: Not found" — half Norwegian, half English machine text — in the same meta row as "7,5 sp" and "fra programmet".
- Fix: data.ts:56-69 — map status to Norwegian sentences instead of echoing the body: 404 → "emnet finnes ikke i katalogen", 429 → "for mange forespørsler, prøv igjen om litt", 5xx → "NTNU svarte ikke", rejected fetch → "ingen nettverkstilkobling". Keep the raw string for `console.debug` only.
- Verifier: Confirmed the worker's English payloads by curl (`{"error":"Not found"}` for an unknown code) and confirmed verbatim rendering by injecting a known string and reading it back out of `#planner-course-rows`. Checked whether some display-layer translation exists between data.ts and the row — there is none, plannerApp.ts:954 concatenates directly. Severity minor: cosmetic, on an already-degraded path, but it is a direct violation of CLAUDE.md's Norwegian-bokmål copy rule on a user-facing surface.
### [plan-2] A period that exists but resolves to nothing is indistinguishable from a normal one, so the planner shows "0 av 30 sp" with no question and no explanation
- Where: `src/components/planner/programPlan.ts:387` · correctness · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `classifyPeriod` returns null only when the period object is absent (line 387-388); a period that exists but yields obligatory: [], choice: [], pendingChoice: null returns an ordinary PeriodCourses. plannerApp.ts:1553 therefore sets periodMissing = false, and weekQuestion() (plannerApp.ts:702-745) has exactly three branches — periodMissing (706), pendingChoice (720) and the U8 elective case which requires pool.length > 0 (735) — so it returns null. Live data reaches this independently of plan-1: `GET /api/program/MPPR/plan?year=2024` period 1 is literally {"periodNumber":1,"direction":{"code":null,"name":null,"courseGroups":[],"waypoints":[]}}. Chromium at /planlegger/#26h;MPPR.2026; : credit line "0 av 30 sp", zero rows, #planner-direction computed display none, grid status empty. The only thing on screen is "Mangler 30 sp" plus the standing "Legg til emne" button.
- Failure: MPPR (Pedagogisk-psykologisk rådgivning) kull 2026, Høst 2026 → a blank planner with "0 av 30 sp" and no sentence anywhere saying the study plan had nothing for this semester; visually identical to a network failure or to never having picked a programme. The periodMissing copy the app already owns ("Studieplanen for kull K har ingen periode for S ennå") is the honest thing to say and is not reachable here.
- Fix: Add a distinguishable state rather than overloading null: an `empty: boolean` on PeriodCourses set when the period resolved to zero courses and zero pendingChoice, plus a fourth weekQuestion() branch — "Studieplanen for kull {cohort} oppgir ingen emner for {semester}. Legg til emnene du tar selv." — mirroring plannerApp.ts:709.
- Verifier: Downgraded from the auditor's major: the student is not stuck — "Mangler 30 sp" and "Legg til emne" are both present and functional, so this is an honesty gap rather than a dead end, and the genuinely-empty-period cause is rare (MPPR p1 is the one I verified myself). I checked it is not merely plan-1 restated: MPPR period 1 has waypoints: [] as well as courseGroups: [], so fixing the nested-waypoint descent would not touch it, and the fix lives in a different function. Also checked renderGapLine's hasContext branch does not compensate — it emits credits, never a reason.
### [plan-4] The S4 "Fant ingen studieplan for kull K" hint is gated on a global constant, so it never fires when a plan really is missing and does fire when one is found
- Where: `src/components/planner/studieinfo.ts:417` · correctness · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `loadCohort` gates the hint on publishedYears.includes(cohort) (studieinfo.ts:417), filled from head.plan.publishedYears (line 375). I pulled publishedYears from five unrelated programmes — MTDT, BSPL, ÅMATSTAT, MSCHEM, MPPR — and every one is byte-identical [2026,2025,…,2007], including the two whose 2026 document 404s. It is the API's global publication window, not a per-programme list, and every cohort relevantCohorts can offer for the current semester (2019-2026) is inside it. Reproduced both failure directions by driving the real modal in Chromium: (a) MPPR + kull 2026 (2026 and 2025 both 404, the 2024 document substituted) → #studieinfo-hint is hidden, empty text; (b) MTDT + Høst 2027 + kull 2027 → hint reads "Fant ingen studieplan for kull 2027 — du kan legge til emner selv." and pressing Lagre immediately produces "30 av 30 sp" (HMS0002, TDT4109, TMA4400, TMA4412, EXPH0300) from the 2026 document. docs/plan/REWORK-2026-07-25-design.md:83-84 requires the opposite behaviour.
- Failure: MPPR/MTMT/MSCHEM kull 2026: the student gets no indication in the modal that their cohort has no plan and that another cohort's curriculum was substituted. MTDT kull 2027 at Høst 2027: the student is told no study plan was found and then handed a complete 30 sp week — the message and the behaviour contradict each other on screen.
- Fix: Stop consulting publishedYears for this decision — findProgramPlan already returns the truth. In loadCohort, compare res.year (or res.plan.year) to cohort: equal → no hint; different → "Fant ingen studieplan for kull {cohort} — viser kull {res.year}. Juster selv."; "kind" in res → the existing not-found copy.
- Verifier: Downgraded from major and I removed the auditor's regression_of: S4. UX-STUDY S4's actual blocker was the kull chips being absent, and that is genuinely fixed (MPPR shows 2026/2025/2024/2023, MTDT shows five chips) — the hint sub-requirement was gated on the wrong field from the start, so this is a never-worked defect, not a regression, and claiming otherwise inflates it. I tried to refute the "global constant" claim by hunting for a programme with a distinct publishedYears; five very different programmes across four studyLevel buckets returned the identical array, and MSCHEM lists 2026 while ?year=2026 404s. Overlaps plan-3 in root cause but is a separate surface with a separate fix.
### [plan-5] The studieinfo modal computes the "this cohort has no such period" dead end and throws it away, so 4 of 5 kull chips silently dead-end when planning a year ahead
- Where: `src/components/planner/studieinfo.ts:507` · ux · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `renderRetning` calls resolvePeriodFor(cohortPlan, stagedSemesterId, stagedCohort) (line 507) and reads only gate.pendingChoice; gate.courses === null — the B4 dead-end signal the planner already has copy for — is discarded and the section is merely hidden. The chips come from relevantCohorts over cohortsPlan, which studieinfo.ts:377-383 deliberately sets to the fullest document (guessYear-6), while each cohort's own document is truncated to what has been published. Verified against the live API for MTDT at Høst 2027: kull 2027 → period 1 (2027 404s, steps back to the 2026 doc which has periods 1,2 — resolves), 2026 → period 3 (2026 doc has 1,2 — absent), 2025 → period 5 (2025 doc has 1-4 — absent), 2024 → period 7 (2024 doc has 1-6 — absent), 2023 → period 9 (2023 doc has 1-8 — absent). Driven by hand in Chromium: selecting kull 2024 with semester "Høst 2027" gives no hint (#studieinfo-hint hidden), no retning section, a normal Lagre; after Lagre the planner says "Ingen periode i studieplanen — Studieplanen for kull 2024 har ingen periode for Høst 2027 ennå…" with "0 av 30 sp" and the programme-sourced courses cleared.
- Failure: An MTDT student planning ahead to Høst 2027 (offered by the semester select — candidateSemesters gives current + next two) picks their own kull from the chip row and saves; four of the five offered chips wipe the semester's programme courses and land on an empty week. The relevance rule (PRODUCT §0 addendum point 10) exists precisely so the chip row carries no dead ends.
- Fix: In renderRetning, when gate.courses === null, set hintText to the sentence the planner already uses ("Studieplanen for kull {cohort} har ingen periode for {semester} ennå") and call renderHint(); optionally aria-disabled the chip. The data is already in hand — no extra fetch.
- Verifier: Tried hard to refute this as "B4's designed honest dead end" — tests/planner/programPlan.test.ts:360 explicitly blesses reporting a missing period, and the post-save state IS honest. What survives is narrower than the auditor claimed and I rewrote the failure scenario accordingly: plans are per-semester (np:plans), so switching to Høst 2027 does not destroy the current semester's plan; the destructive case is only a kull change within an already-populated semester. It stays a real defect because the modal computes the dead end one line above the code that hides it and says nothing, and because the hit rate at the third plannable semester is 4/5 chips, which I verified period-by-period against five MTDT documents.
### [plan-6] A multi-semester thesis is read as a per-semester overload, so the credit note tells a master's student to "fjern det du ikke tar" over one mandatory 60 sp course
- Where: `src/components/planner/plannerApp.ts:610` · copy · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: isSuspiciousPrefill (programPlan.ts:453) flags any obligatory prefill over 30 sp, and renderCreditLine turns that into the fixed sentence at plannerApp.ts:610-613. The study plan attaches a multi-semester course's full credits to its final period: `GET /api/program/MSCHEM/plan?year=2025` lists KJ3900 with credits: null in periods 2 and 3 and credits: 60 in period 4, in all three directions — so the cross-direction intersection prefills it alone. Chromium at /planlegger/#27v;MSCHEM.2025; : credit line "60 av 30 sp" and note "Studieplanen oppgir 60 sp dette semesteret — mer enn et normalt semester. Fjern det du ikke tar." over that single course.
- Failure: An MSCHEM kull 2025 student opening Vår 2027 is told their semester is overloaded and instructed to remove something, when the entire semester is one mandatory master's thesis and there is nothing to remove.
- Fix: Make the sentence conditional on there being something to act on: in renderCreditLine (plannerApp.ts:610-613) emit "Fjern det du ikke tar" only when the obligatory prefill has more than one course; for a single-course overrun say "Studieplanen fører opp hele emnet ({X} sp) i dette semesteret" instead — a >30 sp single course is a multi-semester course, not a study-plan defect.
- Verifier: Reproduced live end-to-end. Checked whether this is the deliberate B9/B9.4 decision and it is not: that decision is about keeping an over-30 sp prefill instead of discarding it (programPlan.ts:445-452, plannerApp.ts:205-215), which is right and which I am not disputing — the defect is only the fixed imperative in the note. I did not independently verify the auditor's "10 of 29 reachable cases" census, so I state only what I reproduced; severity stays minor because the courses themselves are correct.
### [plan-7] Two real programmes can never load a study plan — the worker 400s codes containing "/" or "+", and programPlan's comment claims the opposite
- Where: `worker/src/routes.ts:24` · correctness · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: programPlan.ts:137-139 claims the encoding keeps "the handful of codes carrying a literal '/' ('MSØK/5')" working. It does not: parseCode decodes first (routes.ts:67-76) and then applies CODE_RE = /^[A-ZÆØÅ0-9_-]{2,16}$/i (routes.ts:24), which has no / or +. Live: `GET /api/program/MS%C3%98K%2F5/plan?year=2026` → 400 {"error":"Invalid program code"}; MSECT%2BOH → 400; MTDT → 200. Testing every code in data/programs.json (403 entries) against CODE_RE yields exactly 4 rejects: EMNE/HF, EMNE/SU, MSECT+OH, MSØK/5 — and all 403 are fed to the studieinfo typeahead from that same file (planlegger/index.astro:17-25). The data exists upstream: querying NTNU directly for MSØK/5 cohort 2026 returns a full 9-period plan (SØK1001/1020/1021/1022 in period 1 … SØK3950 masteroppgave in period 10).
- Failure: A 5-year samfunnsøkonomi student searches "Samfunnsøkonomi", picks MSØK/5, and the modal answers "Fant ingen studieplan for dette programmet. Du kan fortsatt legge til emnene dine selv." — permanently, for a programme whose plan upstream serves in full. The 400 comes from our own validator; NTNU is never asked.
- Fix: Add / and + to CODE_RE in worker/src/routes.ts:24 (the decode already runs first and the cache key is built from the decoded value, so nothing else changes), and correct the stale claim in fetchPlan's comment at programPlan.ts:137-139.
- Verifier: Tried to refute on impact: two of the four rejects (EMNE/HF, EMNE/SU) are course collections, not degree programmes, so the real blast radius is 2 programmes, not 4 — which is why I keep this at minor rather than major. Tried to refute on data availability by asking NTNU upstream directly for MSØK/5; the plan came back complete, so the 400 is purely self-inflicted. Confirmed the codes reach the typeahead by reading planlegger/index.astro's programOptions (unfiltered programsData.programs).
### [search-2] On touch there is no clash preview at all on /emner/: the verdict lives only in `title`/`aria-label`, and a tap commits the add before anything is rendered
- Where: `src/pages/emner/index.astro:284` · ux · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `attachClashPreview` writes only `button.title` (284) and `aria-label` (286-289); nothing paints into the row. Reproduced on Pixel 5 (hasTouch true) with np:plans = [TDT4160] and /emner/?q=TDT4110: row text before tap is "TDT4110 | Informasjonsteknologi, grunnkurs | eksamen høst | Trondheim | Legg til", `title` is null. After `tap()`, np:plans immediately contains TDT4110; a DOM scan of every leaf element for /ollisjon|olliderer/ returns []. The verdict is computed (the same run shows `title` becoming "Kolliderer med TDT4160, mandag 14:15." ~2 s after the tap, because pointerenter does fire on touch) — it just has no visible home and lands after commit. Desktop hover on the same row gives "Kolliderer med TDT4160, mandag 14:15.", so the engine is right and only the rendering surface fails. The other two surfaces paint it (addCourse.ts:163 `noteEl.textContent`, emne/[code].astro `clashSlot.replaceChildren`).
- Failure: Phone user with TDT4160 in the plan searches TDT4110 and taps "Legg til": the course is added and the mandag-14:15 collision is never shown on that page — before or after the tap. They see it only by opening /emne/TDT4110/ or the planner grid.
- Fix: Smallest correct change: keep `title`/`aria-label` as-is and additionally write the sentence into a `<span class="emner-row-clash">` appended to `.emner-row-actions` (mirroring addCourse.ts:163-164, including `classList.toggle("is-clash", verdict.kind === "clash")`). Because pointerenter already fires on tap, this alone makes the verdict appear right under the just-toggled button, and the button is a toggle so the add is one tap away from undo. Do NOT add eager/IntersectionObserver prefetching — that is the 200-fetch cost the lazy design exists to avoid (and the popover apparatus UX-STUDY S12's verifier already rejected).
- Verifier: I attacked the auditor's framing and it partly fell: REVIEW.md U11's adjudicated fix says in so many words "the same as `title`/`aria-label` on `/emner/`'s '+' before commit", and PRODUCT.md:246 records the preview as Shipped "computed lazily on first hover/focus" — so calling this a PRODUCT.md §3 violation is wrong and I dropped that category and the major severity. What survived reproduction is narrower and still real: on a touch device the flagship verb produces zero visible output on this page, which neither doc considered. Severity minor because /emne/[code]/ and the planner modal both render it visibly and the add is reversible in one tap.
### [search-3] Missing generation guard lets a stale clash check overwrite a fresh plan with a false "Ingen kollisjon"
- Where: `src/pages/emner/index.astro:266` · correctness · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: Reproduced live, exact sequence: np:plans = [TDT4102], /emner/?q=TDT41, TDT4110's timetable delayed 5 s via page.route. Hover TDT4110 (check starts) → click "Legg til" on TDT4160 → plan change fires syncRows → invalidate correctly blanks the title (observed "" at t=0.9 s) → at t=9 s the stale promise lands and writes title="Ingen kollisjon i planen din for Høst 2026." and aria-label="Legg til TDT4110 i planen — Ingen kollisjon …", while np:plans is [TDT4102, TDT4160]. Ground truth in the same run: moving the pointer away and back re-runs the check and yields "Kolliderer med TDT4160, mandag 14:15." So the stale write is wrong in the safe-looking direction. Cause: `attachClashPreview` (266-300) keeps only `let checked`; `check()` writes unconditionally after `await planClash(...)` and `invalidate()` (296-299) cannot cancel an in-flight promise. The sibling addCourse.ts:140-158 has exactly this guard (`let generation = 0` / `const gen = ++generation` / `if (gen !== generation) return; // superseded`) with a comment describing this very race.
- Failure: Student adds several courses in a row on /emner/. They hover row A, then add row B (which collides with A) within the ~0.2-0.4 s a cold /api/course/*/timetable takes. Row A's button then advertises "Ingen kollisjon i planen din for Høst 2026." in its tooltip and accessible name until a fresh hover/focus completes — so a keyboard/screen-reader user tabbing back to A hears a false all-clear at the moment of focus.
- Fix: Copy the sibling guard into `attachClashPreview` (src/pages/emner/index.astro:266): `let generation = 0;`, `const gen = ++generation;` at the top of `check()`, `if (gen !== generation) return;` immediately after the `await planClash(...)`, and `++generation` inside the returned `invalidate()` alongside `checked = false`.
- Verifier: First attempt to refute succeeded partially and then failed: with an empty plan planClash short-circuits to `{kind:"empty"}` before any fetch (planClash.ts:104), so no race exists — the auditor's window needs a non-empty plan, which I then set up and reproduced verbatim. I also checked the self-healing path: `invalidate()` resets `checked`, so a later pointerenter/focus does recompute the correct verdict — that is why I downgraded major → minor. The exposure that remains is the interval between the stale write and the next completed check, during which both the tooltip and the accessible name assert a collision-free plan that collides.
### [search-4] The query is never written to the URL, so Back from a course page returns to an empty catalog
- Where: `src/pages/emner/index.astro:495` · ux · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: Lines 495-497 read `?q=` into the input on setup, but the file contains no `history.replaceState`/`pushState` at all (grep over src/ finds them only in plannerApp.ts). Driven live: /emner/ → type "algoritmer" (status "5 treff", url still http://127.0.0.1:8788/emner/) → click row 1 → http://127.0.0.1:8788/emne/IDATA2302/ → `page.goBack()` → url http://127.0.0.1:8788/emner/, `#emner-search` value "", `#emner-results li` count 0, status "Skriv for å søke i 5470 emner." Deep links do work (/emner/?q=maskinlæring → "19 treff", 19 rows), which is what makes the write-side omission cheap to fix.
- Failure: Student searches "matematikk", scrolls to TMA4100, opens it, decides against it and presses Back: query, 112 results and scroll position are all gone. Comparing three candidates means retyping the query three times.
- Fix: At the end of `render()` in src/pages/emner/index.astro, `history.replaceState(null, "", trimmed === "" ? location.pathname : `?q=${encodeURIComponent(trimmed)}`)`. The existing read path at 495-497 already restores it on Back, so no other change is needed; persisting facets as `&sted=` is optional.
- Verifier: Tried to kill it via ClientRouter: Astro's view transitions could plausibly restore the input/DOM on history traversal, so I ran the full click-and-Back cycle rather than trusting the code read — the restored page is genuinely blank (0 rows, empty input). Also checked e2e/navigation.pw.ts:155-177, which drives exactly this page and this query but never goes Back, so nothing guards it. Downgraded major → minor: the state is recoverable by retyping and nothing is wrong or lost, it is friction — but friction multiplied by search-1's scroll depth.
### [search-5] `fold()` normalizes Norwegian one way only, and any space in the query kills the match: "TDT 4100" and "maskinlaering" both return 0 treff
- Where: `src/pages/emner/index.astro:193` · correctness · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `fold()` (193-200) lowercases, maps æ→a, ø→o, then NFD-strips (å→a); `render()` matches with a single `String.includes` of the whole trimmed query (318). Live: /emner/?q=TDT%204100 → "0 treff", 0 rows, while ?q=TDT4100 → 1 treff; ?q=maskinlaering → "0 treff" while ?q=maskinlæring → "19 treff" (19 rows). Offline replay of the same fold+filter over the 5470-course index: "oekonomi" 0 vs "okonomi"/"økonomi" 136; "datastrukturer algoritmer" 0 vs "algoritmer og datastrukturer" 3. The code's own comment (190-192) states the intent — "'okonomi' has to find 'Økonomi' on a Norwegian site" — so the missing reverse direction is an oversight, not a decision.
- Failure: A student on a non-Norwegian keyboard types "maskinlaering" and is told 0 treff although 19 courses match; a student who types the code as printed on a timetable, "TDT 4100", is told 0 treff for a course that exists. In both cases the zero state gives no hint that spelling or spacing is the problem.
- Fix: Two independent one-liners in src/pages/emner/index.astro. (1) In `fold()`, after the diacritic strip add `.replace(/ae/g, "a").replace(/oe/g, "o").replace(/aa/g, "a")` — applied to both sides it stays symmetric. (2) In `render()`, split the folded query on `/\s+/` and require every token to appear in `fold(code) + " " + fold(name)`; that alone fixes "TDT 4100" and word-order variants. Note: it does NOT fix "matte 1" (the auditor's fix claims it does — "matte" is not a substring of "matematikk"); abbreviation/synonym matching is a separate, larger question, do not build it here.
- Verifier: Verified both halves live rather than by reading, and checked the two escape hatches: no synonym/alias table exists anywhere (grep), and the planner's own search modal shares the same fold via a separate copy, so there is no compensating surface. I also verified the proposed digraph fix cannot create false negatives (it folds both query and index identically), only rare extra matches. Kept at minor, not major: the exact-code path ("TDT4100") works, and a Norwegian student on a Norwegian keyboard — the primary user — hits neither case.
### [search-7] Typing with the pointer parked over the button column fires two upstream NTNU calls per re-render, for courses nobody chose to inspect
- Where: `src/pages/emner/index.astro:291` · perf · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: Reproduced live and counted at the network layer: search "TDT4120", park the mouse on its "Legg til" (x≈1171, y≈335 — 4 API calls, the candidate plus the plan partner, as designed), then focus the field via JS (no pointer movement) and type "materialteknologi" one character at a time → 10 further /api/ requests for 5 courses that were never deliberately hovered: AAR1025, AAR4436, AAR4546, IMAK2011, IMAK3001 (each `/api/course/X/timetable?year=2026&version=1` + `/api/course/X`). Cause: `render()` rebuilds every row on every `input` (321-324) and `check` is bound to bare `pointerenter` (291) with no dwell, so whichever new button lands under the stationary cursor gets a boundary event. `fetchCourseBundle`'s memo dedupes repeats of one course, not a stream of distinct ones. CLAUDE.md's own rule for the crawler is "keep it polite"; this is the same upstream.
- Failure: A student adds a course, then types their next query without lifting the mouse: a dozen requests go through the Worker to NTNU for arbitrary courses whose verdicts are never seen — on a metered phone tether, and against an upstream this repo otherwise treats carefully.
- Fix: In src/pages/emner/index.astro:291, gate `check()` behind a dwell: on `pointerenter` start a ~200 ms `setTimeout`, clear it on `pointerleave`/`pointercancel`, and clear it in the teardown that `attachClashPreview` already returns. Leave `focus` immediate (it is the keyboard equivalent of a deliberate hover).
- Verifier: I expected this to die on the memo in lib/planner/data.ts or on Chromium not firing boundary events without mouse movement — neither saved it; I counted the requests myself with a `request` listener and got 10 for 5 unintended courses. I did not keep the auditor's second data point (tabbing 24 stops → 22 requests): focus-triggered checks are the documented keyboard path for the preview and are deliberate, so that half is not a defect. Minor, not major: nothing breaks and the volume is bounded by how many distinct rows pass under the cursor.
### [sec-1] Shared-plan hash writes an unvalidated programme "code" into localStorage, and it then renders as site chrome on every page
- Where: `src/lib/planner/store.ts:662` · security · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: Traced and reproduced. `parsePlanHash` (store.ts:650-675) validates only `semesterId` (`SEMESTER_ID_PATTERN`, line 656) and `cohort` (`cohortIsPlausible`, line 665); `code`/`directionRaw` are taken verbatim (lines 662-670). `parseHashToken` (store.ts:566-587) likewise accepts any non-empty `codeRaw` (lines 584-586). `planFromHash` (plannerApp.ts:361-372) copies the string into both `PlanProgram.code` and `.name`; plannerApp.ts:412 `store.savePlan(plan)` persists it, and `savePlan` (store.ts:297) writes `np:profile`. Layout.astro:218-227 renders `plan.program.code` into `#studieinfo-chip` on EVERY page. Live Playwright probe against 127.0.0.1:8787 with `#26h;ADVARSEL%3A%20kontoen%20er%20sperret%20-%20ring%20800%2012%20345.2024;TDT4100`: chip on /planlegger/, then on /emner/ and / (no hash on either) all read `ADVARSEL: kontoen er sperret - ring 800 12 345 · 2024 · Høst 2026`; `np:profile` = `{"program":{"code":"ADVARSEL…","name":"ADVARSEL…","cohort":2024}}`; screenshot v-poison-home.png shows the homepage resume link rendering `Planen din: ADVARSEL: kontoen er sperret - ring 800 12 345 · 1 emne → gå til planleggeren` in full accent underlined type. 0 dialogs — text injection, not XSS. No test covers it: tests/planner/store.test.ts:470-622 exercises the grammar (`#banana`, `#v2;…`, empty tokens) but never an implausible code shape, and REVIEW C4/B10.2's fixes stopped at semesterId + cohort.
- Failure: A student is sent a plan link in a study-group chat (link sharing is the product's own flow — the homepage says "Del planen med en lenke — ingen innlogging"). Opening `…/planlegger/#26h;<attacker text>.2024;TDT4100` writes that text to `np:profile`; from then on the topbar chip and the homepage "Planen din" line show the attacker's message on every page, on every visit, with no hash in the URL. Nothing hints it came from a link, and only "Fjern studieprogram" inside the studieinfo modal clears it.
- Fix: In `src/lib/planner/store.ts`, add a code-shape guard and apply it in `parsePlanHash`'s programme branch (lines 662-670) and `parseHashToken` (lines 584-586), and in `coerceProfile`/`coerceCourse` so an already-poisoned `np:profile` is dropped on read. CORRECTION to the auditor's fix: do NOT reuse the worker's `/^[A-ZÆØÅ0-9_-]{2,16}$/i` for the direction — real study-plan codes run longer (`/api/program/MTDT/plan?year=2024` contains `MTDTDSÅR3EMNER-24` (17) and `SIVING-ARBERFARING24` (20)); use `{2,16}` for the programme/course code and a wider `{2,32}` character-class bound for the direction, or the fix breaks valid shared links.
- Verifier: Attacked it three ways. (1) Looked for a guard downstream: `coerceProfile` (store.ts:163-186) only type-checks `string`/`number`, no shape check — no rescue. (2) Checked whether the worker's `CODE_RE` rejection saves the UI: it doesn't — the study-plan fetch 400s, so the `namedProgram` backfill (plannerApp.ts:1560-1578) never fires and the raw string stays as both code AND name. (3) Checked REVIEW.md for a prior fixed item: C4/B10.2 (docs/REVIEW.md:338-349, 588) added exactly the semesterId and cohort checks and stopped there — this is an uncovered adjacent case, not a regression. Survived. I DOWNGRADED major→minor: no XSS (auditor concedes; I reproduced 0 dialogs/0 injected nodes), the victim must open an attacker-supplied link, the chip visibly truncates the payload, and the text lands in a slot the UI labels as a programme code. Persistent content spoofing, not a state a real user reaches on their own.
### [sec-3] No security response headers on any route — no CSP, nosniff, Referrer-Policy or frame-ancestors
- Where: `worker/src/server.ts:49` · security · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: Live `curl -s -D -` against 127.0.0.1:8787 (note: the brief's :8788 is stale; wrangler is bound to :8787): `/` and `/planlegger/` return only Transfer-Encoding, Content-Type, Cache-Control, ETag, CF-Cache-Status; `/data/search-index.json` returns `Content-Type: application/json` with no `nosniff`; `/api/course/TDT4100` returns only Content-Type + Cache-Control. `worker/src/server.ts:48-50` passes `env.ASSETS.fetch(request)` straight through untouched; `json()`/`errorJson()` (worker/src/routes.ts:31-53) set only content-type and Cache-Control. A repo-wide grep for `Content-Security|X-Frame|X-Content-Type|Referrer-Policy|nosniff` over src/worker/public/docs/e2e/astro.config.mjs/wrangler.jsonc returns zero hits, and there is no `public/_headers` (public/ contains only `data/`). CSP is cheap here: `dist/planlegger/index.html`, `dist/index.html` and `dist/emne/TDT4100/index.html` each contain exactly ONE executable inline `<script>` (Layout's no-flash theme block, identical across pages) plus hashed `/_astro/*.js` modules; the second inline tag on /planlegger/ is `type="application/json"`, never executed.
- Failure: Any future injection slip (sec-1 already shows attacker-controlled strings reaching persistent state and the DOM) executes with nothing to contain it; `/data/search-index.json` is sniffable; and any page can be framed for click-jacking of destructive controls ("Fjern studieprogram", "Dropp") since nothing sends `X-Frame-Options`/`frame-ancestors`.
- Fix: In `worker/src/server.ts`, wrap the `env.ASSETS.fetch(request)` return (line 49) in a helper that clones the response and adds `Content-Security-Policy`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin` and `X-Frame-Options: DENY`; add `nosniff` to `json()`/`errorJson()` in routes.ts. CORRECTION to the auditor's policy string: `style-src 'self'` alone WILL break the site — `dist/index.html` still ships one Astro scoped `<style>` block and the build emits two `style="…"` attributes; use `style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'` (or hash the block) while keeping `script-src 'self' 'sha256-…'`. Pin it with one e2e assertion so a future inline script fails loudly.
- Verifier: Re-ran the header probe myself on the correct port and confirmed the absence on four route classes. Tried to kill it by looking for the headers being applied elsewhere — no `public/_headers`, nothing in `wrangler.jsonc`, nothing in `astro.config.mjs` — so this is not a local-dev artefact. Also stress-tested the recommended fix and found it would break the build's own inline `<style>`, hence the corrected policy above. Severity stays minor and is defense-in-depth only: the site has no accounts, no cookies, no sensitive data, and the click-jacking payoff is a destroyable local plan.
### [sec-4] Worker returns the raw upstream error string — full NTNU Liferay portlet URL — and the planner prints it verbatim as Norwegian UI copy
- Where: `worker/src/routes.ts:93` · security · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: Traced end to end. `mapUpstreamError` (worker/src/routes.ts:85-96) maps `NotFoundError`→"Not found" and `RateLimitError`→"Rate limited", but any other `NTNUAPIError` returns `errorJson(err.message, 502)` (line 93). `ntnu-api`'s `statusErrorFromResponse` builds that message as `${method} ${url} -> ${response.status}` with the final URL including every query param (node_modules/ntnu-api/dist/errors.js:80-95), and `HttpClient.request` throws it at dist/http.js:146 and :156; `requestJson` throws `InvalidResponseError` carrying the URL plus the upstream `Content-Type` (dist/http.js:178-179), and `APIConnectionError` embeds the URL too (dist/http.js:160). `CoursesClient.timetable` (dist/courses.js:124-125) does not catch. Client side: `fetchJson` copies `body.error` into `Error.message` (src/lib/planner/data.ts:56-68), line 110 pushes `timeplan: <that>` into `bundle.errors`, and plannerApp.ts:953-955 renders `fikk ikke hentet ${error}` into the course rail.
- Failure: NTNU's Liferay portlet 5xx's (the same flaky surface the retry layer exists for). Every affected row in the planner rail then reads `fikk ikke hentet timeplan: GET https://www.ntnu.no/web/studier/emner?p_p_id=coursedetailsportlet_WAR_courselistportlet&…&year=2026&version=1 -> 500` — a ~250-character English machine URL where a Norwegian explanation should be, in the one place (DR-6 honest-gap copy) the product stakes its credibility on.
- Fix: In `worker/src/routes.ts:93`, return `errorJson("Upstream error", 502)` and `console.warn(err.message)` so the detail stays in Workers Logs (observability is already on in wrangler.jsonc); optionally map the 502 in `src/lib/planner/data.ts` to a Norwegian sentence so the rail reads `fikk ikke hentet timeplan: NTNU svarte ikke`.
- Verifier: Attacked the reachability rather than trusting the auditor's node repro: read `statusErrorFromResponse` and confirmed the message template literally embeds the final URL, confirmed `NotFoundError`/`RateLimitError` are intercepted first (so the common 404 path does NOT leak), and confirmed `courses.timetable` has no internal catch. I could not force a live 5xx from NTNU without hammering upstream, so I did not reproduce the rendered string end to end — but every hop is a direct assignment with no sanitisation, so the trace is conclusive. Severity minor: the endpoint is a public scraped URL, so the disclosure is weak; the real cost is broken UI copy at the moment the product most needs to sound trustworthy.
### [sec-5] The `/api` proxy has no throttle and no bounded key space: any novel code/year/version triggers an uncached upstream fetch to NTNU
- Where: `worker/src/routes.ts:155` · security · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: Live timings on the running worker (127.0.0.1:8787). Version dimension: `/api/course/TDT4110/timetable?year=2026&version=1` → 0.232 s then 0.006 s (cached); `version=874512` → 0.222 s then 0.002 s — every novel version string is exactly one uncached upstream request, since `parseVersion` (routes.ts:155-159) validates nothing beyond "not empty" and the value goes into both the cache key (routes.ts:173) and `client.courses.timetable(..., { version })` (routes.ts:178). But the code and year dimensions behave identically and are far larger: `/api/course/ZQXW1234/timetable?year=2026` (a nonexistent code) → 0.564 s upstream, 0.002 s cached; `ZQXW9999` → 0.183 s upstream; `/api/course/TDT4110/timetable?year=2011` → 0.396 s and `year=2012` → 0.221 s, both upstream. `CODE_RE` admits 16 chars of `[A-ZÆØÅ0-9_-]` and `YEAR_RE` any 4-digit year. There is no rate limiting anywhere in `worker/src/server.ts`, and `ntnu-api`'s HttpClient retries 429/502/503/504 (dist/http.js:120-156), so one inbound request can become up to three upstream ones.
- Failure: A loop of `curl "https://<site>/api/course/$RANDOMCODE/timetable?year=2026"` from one machine — or `fetch(…, {mode:'no-cors'})` embedded on a third-party page — produces an unbounded stream of uncacheable requests to www.ntnu.no all originating from this worker's egress. For an explicitly unofficial site, the realistic outcome is NTNU blocking the worker and the site dying.
- Fix: The smallest honest mitigation is NOT parameter validation (see verify_note) but a request cap: a per-IP Cloudflare rate-limiting rule, or a cheap in-isolate token bucket keyed on `CF-Connecting-IP`, in front of the `/api/*` branch at worker/src/server.ts:48-86. Separately, tighten `parseVersion` (routes.ts:155-159) to `/^\d{1,3}$/` as hygiene — it removes the unbounded KV-key path (`worker/src/cache.ts:125` would throw on KV's 512-byte key limit for a ~450-char version) — but do not mistake it for the fix.
- Verifier: I killed the auditor's framing and kept the underlying fact. Their claim was that `?version=` is what makes this an uncapped-key proxy and that `/^\d{1,3}$/` "caps the key space at 1000 per code+year". I refuted that live: bogus course codes and arbitrary 4-digit years each already produce uncached upstream fetches (0.18–0.56 s vs 0.002 s cached), so the code×year space dwarfs the version space and the proposed fix reduces abuse potential by essentially nothing. I also killed the "permanent cache bypass" sub-claim: `TieredCache.set` (cache.ts:121-132) writes memory FIRST and catches the KV failure, so an oversized key still caches per-isolate. What survives is the broader, real gap — no throttle of any kind on an unauthenticated proxy to a third party the project has committed to treating politely — so I kept it at minor with a corrected title, mechanism and fix rather than reporting the version parameter as the vector.
### [store-4] A plain F5 rewrites the plan from the hash, which carries no `credits` and no `name` — destroying the B9.1 study-plan credit fallback on disk
- Where: `src/components/planner/plannerApp.ts:404` · data · CONFIRMED · regression of REVIEW.md B9.1
- Evidence: The hash-wins branch (plannerApp.ts:400-412) replaces the loaded plan with `planFromHash(...)`, which rebuilds every course as `{code, name: c.code, version, source, dropped?, groups?}` (381-390) because `formatHashToken`/`HashCourse` (store.ts:590-610) carry neither field — then `store.savePlan(plan)` (412) persists the impoverished version. Since the page always writes its own hash (`syncHash`, 441-444), this fires on every reload of the same tab. Reproduced live (scratchpad/v2/e.mjs) with `np:plans` seeded `[{TMA4100,"Matematikk 1",manual,credits:7.5},{ZZZ9999,"Fordypningsemne i studieplanen",manual,credits:15}]`: first load renders both names with "7,5 sp"/"15 sp" and `#planner-credit-line` = "22,5 av 30 sp". After `reload()` the rows read "TMA4100 | Matematikk 1 | (no sp)" and "ZZZ9999 | ZZZ9999 | (no sp)", the credit line reads "0 av 30 sp (+2 emner uten oppgitt sp)", and `np:plans` is `[{TMA4100,"Matematikk 1",manual},{ZZZ9999,"ZZZ9999",manual}]` — credits gone from disk. This is B9.1's class on live data: of MTDT's 43 study-plan course references, 3 (TMA4100, TMA4140, TMA4115) have `offeredYears` without 2026 in public/data/search-index.json and 404 from /api/course (the probe row shows "fikk ikke hentet detaljer: Not found"); 703 of 5470 index rows are in that state.
- Failure: A student adds an elective from the "Fra studieplanen" panel (buildPlanPanelRow, plannerApp.ts:1011-1016, the only path that passes `credits`) whose code is not in this year's catalog — the study plan supplied its 7,5 sp and its name, and the credit line reads "30 av 30 sp". She presses F5. The row loses its sp (and, if the code is outside the two-year index union, its name too) and the credit line drops to "22,5 av 30 sp (+1 emne uten oppgitt sp)" permanently.
- Fix: src/components/planner/plannerApp.ts, hash-wins branch (line 404): merge instead of replace — key the stored plan's courses by code and carry `credits`/`name` over onto the hash-derived course of the same code before `store.savePlan(plan)`. Smaller and safer than widening the frozen course-token grammar (PRODUCT §7/D15), which would permit an additive `!<credits>` suffix but is not needed to stop the data loss.
- Verifier: I tried hard to kill this. (a) Names: mostly refuted — the search-index backfill at plannerApp.ts:1654-1664 restores real names for any code in the two-catalog-year union, and my probe confirmed TMA4100 kept "Matematikk 1" while only the synthetic ZZZ9999 degraded, so I removed the name half from the severity calculus. (b) Credits on programme courses: refuted — `setProgramPlan` re-derives them and `sameProgramSet` compares credits (plannerApp.ts:234-247), so they come back. (c) Credits on manual adds: survives, but only bites when /api/course cannot supply them, i.e. catalog-absent courses added from the "Fra studieplanen" panel, the only caller that passes `credits` at all; I confirmed such courses exist in a real MTDT plan (3 of 43). Because the UI still labels the shortfall honestly ("+1 emne uten oppgitt sp") and the reachable population is small, I downgraded major → minor and re-anchored from the grammar (store.ts:590) to the clobbering write (plannerApp.ts:404).
### [store-5] A stored/shared group key that matches no timetable entry blanks the course's blocks, and the week then blames NTNU: "Ingen timeplandata … ennå"
- Where: `src/lib/planner/groups.ts:157` · correctness · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `applyGroupSelection` (groups.ts:157-163) treats a non-empty `selected` as absolute — it keeps only entries whose `groupKey` is in the set, plus ungrouped ones, with no fallback when the set matches nothing — and the keys come straight out of the hash/localStorage (`parseHashToken`, store.ts:566-588) with no validation against the fetched timetable. Reproduced live myself (scratchpad/v2/e.mjs): `/planlegger/#26h;-;%2BTDT4136~forelesning-3` (TDT4136's real 2026 group titles are "Main lecture", "Assignment lecture", "Lab hour for assignments 1-4", so the key matches nothing) yields 0 `.planner-block` elements and `#planner-grid-frame` reading "Ingen timeplandata for emnene i planen ennå." — while the same page's exam ribbon and course row, fed by the same successful fetch, prove the data arrived. The baseline `#26h;-;%2BTDT4136` renders 2 blocks.
- Failure: A student picks "Forelesningsparallell 2" in the block popover; the key lands in her URL and localStorage. NTNU republishes that course's timetable with retitled groups (TDT4136's titles are currently English while the UI is Norwegian, so a language flip alone would do it). She reopens her own link and that course's blocks are simply absent from the week; if it is her only course the app asserts NTNU has no timetable data at all.
- Fix: src/components/planner/plannerApp.ts: after a bundle lands in `loadBundles`, reconcile `state.course.groups` against `groupOptions(timetable).map(o => o.key)` and call `store.setCourseGroups(code, matched)` (`[]` when none match, restoring the documented defaults). Belt and braces in groups.ts:157 — if a non-empty `selected` matches zero grouped entries, fall through to the default branch instead of returning an empty list.
- Verifier: The mechanism I re-reproduced myself; the trigger is what I attacked. I traced every in-app path that could write a key: the popover writes keys derived from the live timetable (valid at write time); `setProgramPlan` preserves keys per course code across a programme change but the key belongs to that course's own timetable, so it stays valid; plans are semester-scoped (`np:plans` keyed by semester id), so a semester switch cannot carry keys into a different timetable; summer semesters are filtered out of the candidate set (plannerApp.ts:188-196) so no exotic id path exists. That leaves only NTNU retitling groups between the pick and the revisit, whose frequency I could not establish. Kept, but downgraded major → minor on that basis.
### [store-7] An uppercase semester id passes the grammar's case-insensitive check but fails `knownSemester`, producing a false "we can't plan that semester yet" apology in the one line whose job is honesty
- Where: `src/lib/planner/store.ts:657` · correctness · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `SEMESTER_ID_PATTERN = /^\d{2}[hv]$/i` (store.ts:522) deliberately accepts either case, but line 657 returns `decodeField(semesterRaw)` verbatim with no `toLowerCase()`, while `knownSemester` (plannerApp.ts:356-358) compares `s.id === id` against the lowercase ids in data/semesters.json (I dumped them: `28h,28s,28v,27h,…,26h,…`). Reproduced live (scratchpad/v2/e.mjs): `/planlegger/#26H;MTDT.2024;TDT4136` → `#planner-link-note` reads "Lenken pekte på et semester vi ikke kan planlegge ennå — viser Høst 2026." while `#planner-context-line` reads "kull 2024 · Høst 2026" — the note apologises for not being able to plan the very semester it is showing. Lowercase `#26h;…` produces an empty link note.
- Failure: A shared link passes through anything that uppercases the fragment (a hand-typed `#26H`, a mobile keyboard's autocapitalise). Best case the recipient is told the sender's link pointed at an unplannable semester while that exact semester is on screen; worse case a `#27V` link is silently substituted with Høst 2026 and the recipient plans the wrong term.
- Fix: src/lib/planner/store.ts:657 — `const semesterId = decodeField(semesterRaw).toLowerCase();`. `formatPlanHash` already writes the stored lowercase id, so nothing else changes.
- Verifier: I looked for a normaliser anywhere between the parse and `knownSemester` — `planFromHash` (plannerApp.ts:361-392) passes `parsed.semesterId` through untouched and there is no `toLowerCase` in either file's semester path. Then I reproduced both the uppercase and lowercase cases live and diffed the link note. I considered refuting it as unreachable (nothing in the app writes uppercase) but kept it at minor because the failure is a false statement in the C4 honesty line, the one surface that must never lie, and the fix is a single method call.
### [tests-1] e2e.yml's five trigger globs miss the two files CLAUDE.md calls load-bearing — HEAD~1 edited Layout.astro's script and fired nothing
- Where: `.github/workflows/e2e.yml:17` · test-coverage · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: Verified the globs (e2e.yml lines 18-22 and 26-30): src/lib/planner/**, src/components/planner/**, worker/**, e2e/**, playwright.config.ts. `ls src/lib/` shows pageLifecycle.ts sits at src/lib/, NOT src/lib/planner/ — outside the glob. src/layouts/**, src/pages/** and src/components/site/** (courseDetails.ts, courseTimetable.ts, gradeChart.ts, planClash.ts) are all unwatched, yet e2e/navigation.pw.ts's own header names its subjects as Layout.astro's `astro:after-swap` re-apply and the onPage() wrapper, and it asserts #studieinfo-chip at lines 196-207 and data-theme at 74-103. Empirically scored the last commits: `git show --name-only 665513f` = src/layouts/Layout.astro + src/styles/site.css, 0 of 2 matching any glob, and its message says it removed "its render branch in Layout's script (the block now only maintains the chip)" — the exact block navigation.pw.ts guards. 4664fae and 93c24c5 also score 0. No unit test covers Layout.astro or pageLifecycle.ts (tests/ holds only planner/, site/, worker/, crawler, fonts). e2e.yml's own header claims it is "Scoped to the paths where a regression is plausible", which contradicts CLAUDE.md's naming of onPage() and the after-swap re-apply as the two load-bearing invariants.
- Failure: A developer edits src/layouts/Layout.astro's astro:after-swap handler or src/lib/pageLifecycle.ts's onPage(). ci.yml runs lint/typecheck/vitest/build — all green, because no unit test touches either file. e2e.yml does not trigger. main is now broken: every in-site navigation drops data-theme (site snaps to light) or leaves the planner an empty shell, and the only suite that can see it never ran. main stays red-but-green-badged until the nightly cron or the next tag.
- Fix: .github/workflows/e2e.yml — widen both `paths:` lists to `src/**`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `wrangler.jsonc` (or simply drop the path filter; the suite is ~26 tests on one worker). At minimum add `src/layouts/**` and widen `src/lib/planner/**` → `src/lib/**` so the two CLAUDE.md invariants are covered.
- Verifier: Attacked it as a documented deliberate decision — CLAUDE.md does bless path scoping, but only conditionally ("Do not narrow the trigger paths without checking whether the change you're making could regress the flagship flow through a path that's no longer watched"), and the omission of the two files CLAUDE.md itself names is an internal contradiction, not a decision. Attacked the impact: release.yml re-runs the full e2e suite on every tag regardless of the tagged diff's paths, so a regression can never reach a deploy — that is why I DOWNGRADED the auditor's `major` to minor. The exposure is a red main between merge and the next tag/nightly, not a user-facing defect. The empirical HEAD~1 evidence I re-derived myself and it holds exactly as claimed.
### [tests-2] Five e2e assertions budget 45 s inside tests Playwright caps at 30 s, so the cold-cache headroom their authors wrote is unreachable
- Where: `playwright.config.ts:12` · test-coverage · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: I proved the default rather than assuming it: wrote a throwaway config with no `timeout` and a test printing testInfo.timeout, ran it in the scratchpad (no server touched) — output `TEST_TIMEOUT=30000` on @playwright/test 1.61.1. playwright.config.ts sets fullyParallel, workers, reporter, use, projects and webServer.timeout: 240_000 but no top-level `timeout` and no `expect.timeout`. `grep -n timeout e2e/*.pw.ts` shows five `{ timeout: 45_000 }` assertions in flows.pw.ts at lines 85, 203, 221, 354 and 381, each commented as needing headroom for a cold worker cache. Summed sequential allowances inside single tests: onboarding (flows.pw.ts:48-88) = 15_000+15_000+20_000+20_000+45_000 = 115 000 ms; "groups: a non-default parallel" (191-223) = 30_000+45_000+45_000 = 120 000 ms; "share: the hash reproduces the plan" (90-108) = 90 000 ms.
