Every one is capped at 30 000 ms, so the test dies with "Test timeout of 30000ms exceeded" before any 45 s allowance can expire.
- Failure: On a GitHub-hosted runner the worker cache is memory-only and cold (no KV binding — wrangler.jsonc's kv_namespaces block is still commented out), so the onboarding flow issues a programme search, a study-plan call and 5 course bundles straight to ntnu.no. If that chain plus Chromium startup exceeds 30 s — the exact case the 45 s was written for — the flagship 'programme + kull → your week' test fails red on a healthy build. release.yml runs `npm run test:e2e` as a hard gate before `wrangler deploy`, so the spurious red blocks the deploy and the failure message points at the grid rather than at the timeout budget.
- Fix: playwright.config.ts — add `timeout: 120_000` to defineConfig (optionally `expect: { timeout: 15_000 }`) so a test's budget exceeds the assertion budgets its author wrote. No spec change needed.
- Verifier: Verified the default empirically instead of trusting the auditor (30 000 ms confirmed on the installed 1.61.1) and re-derived the three sums from the actual line numbers. Then attacked the severity, and it does not hold at major: this defect can only ever produce a FALSE RED, never a false green — a test that dies at 30 s fails, it does not silently pass. In a dimension about CI trustworthiness that makes it a latent flake and a deploy-blocking annoyance, not a trust hole, and the suite currently passes (26 passed in 94b5d9a), so nothing is failing today. Downgraded major → minor. Kept because the authors explicitly wrote the headroom and the harness silently discards it.
### [tests-5] CI's crawl-data cache copies itself forward forever — after the first cold run, ci.yml and e2e.yml never crawl again
- Where: `.github/workflows/e2e.yml:52` · data · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: Traced the mechanism through the YAML and the script. Both ci.yml:32-33 and e2e.yml:52-53 use `key: crawl-data-${today}` with `restore-keys: crawl-data-`, then run `node crawler/ensure-data.mjs`. I read ensure-data.mjs in full: it does `existsSync` on the four artifact paths and, if none are missing, prints "all crawl artifacts present, skipping crawl" and exits 0 — no freshness check, no year check. actions/cache saves at post-job whenever the PRIMARY key missed, so day N restores day N-1's bytes through the prefix key, skips the crawl because the files exist, and re-saves those same bytes under day N's key. By induction the content is frozen at the first cold run; the daily re-save also keeps refreshing GitHub's 7-day last-accessed clock, so it never ages out either. ci.yml's own comment ("keeps CI at zero-to-one real crawls per day") describes behaviour the configuration does not implement — it is zero-to-one crawls in total. crawl.yml no-ops entirely while CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN is unset (verified: every step is gated on `env.HAS_DEPLOY_CREDS == 'true'`), leaving release.yml's `npm run crawl` as the only refresh path that runs.
- Failure: e2e.yml's nightly cron exists, per its own header, because "live upstream data drifts … and only a schedule catches that". Half the inputs are baked, not live: data/catalog.json, data/programs.json, data/semesters.json and public/data/search-index.json. When NTNU rolls to the 2027 catalog, the nightly run keeps building from the frozen index — /emner/ search misses every new course, /emne/<new code>/ pages are never generated, and semesters.json's `current` names an elapsed term — while every e2e test still passes, because the specs assert against the live /api half. release.yml crawls fresh, so the artifact CI validated and the artifact that deploys are different sites.
- Fix: Give crawler/ensure-data.mjs a freshness guard (re-crawl when data/catalog.json's crawledAt is older than ~24 h, or when its `year` is not the current canonical year) and drop `restore-keys: crawl-data-` from .github/workflows/e2e.yml so a stale prefix hit cannot satisfy the day's key. ci.yml may keep the loose restore-key — stale data really is fine for lint/typecheck/build.
- Verifier: Tried to kill it on actions/cache semantics: checked whether a restore-key (partial) hit suppresses the post-job save — it does not; only an exact primary-key match does, so the stale bytes are re-saved under the new key every run. Tried to kill it on GitHub's 7-day eviction — the daily prefix restore + re-save keeps the newest entry perpetually fresh, so eviction never breaks the chain. Tried to kill it as harmless: it IS harmless today (no remote exists yet, and local artifacts are real — public/data/search-index.json is year 2026 with 5 470 courses), which is why minor is the right severity; the defect is in what CI will do the moment it starts running. Could not refute the mechanism.
### [ux-7] Raw upstream/technical strings render verbatim in the week: an undecoded "&#38;" in real block labels and an English "Failed to fetch" in the Norwegian course rail
- Where: `src/lib/planner/data.ts:110` · copy · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: (a) Reproduced live at /planlegger/#26h;MTELSYS.2026;: the student's actual Monday 08:15 block renders innerText "TMA4400 ~ F1 · 08:15 ~ Forelesning 1 MTELSYS &#38; MTTK ~ uke 34–47" and title="TMA4400 · Forelesning 1 MTELSYS &#38; MTTK · 08:15–10:00 · F1 · uke 34–47" — an undecoded HTML numeric entity where "&" belongs. The raw payload confirms it: /api/course/TMA4400/timetable carries `"title":"Forelesning 1 MTELSYS &#38; MTTK"`. It also appears in the block popover's group-picker option list. This is in the week itself, not only the popover — every MTELSYS/MTTK first-year sees it. (b) data.ts:110 does `errors.push(`timeplan: ${timetableResult.reason?.message ?? …}`)` and plannerApp.ts:954 renders it as `fikk ikke hentet ${error}`; with the timetable route aborted the course rows read "fikk ikke hentet timeplan: Failed to fetch" (reproduced twice: MTDT 2024 full abort, MTDT 2026 single-course abort).
- Failure: An MTELSYS or MTTK first-year opens their week and their Monday lecture is labelled "Forelesning 1 MTELSYS &#38; MTTK"; a student whose connection drops sees an English JavaScript exception message inside a Norwegian bokmål course list.
- Fix: (a) Decode numeric/named HTML entities once where the timetable bundle is mapped in data.ts (a small `decodeEntities` applied to `title`/`name`), so the block label, the title attribute and the popover option all get "&". (b) At plannerApp.ts:954 render only the token before the colon — `fikk ikke hentet ${error.split(":")[0]}` — exactly what renderProvenance already does at plannerApp.ts:1414, giving "fikk ikke hentet timeplan" while keeping the underlying message for debugging.
- Verifier: Verified (a) and (b) live rather than from the auditor's screenshots, and found (a) is more reachable than claimed — it renders in the week's own block label and title attribute, not just the popover. Killed the auditor's third sub-claim: TDT4136's "Main lecture"/"Assignment lecture" is upstream's own activity title rendered verbatim, matching the codebase's consistent policy of never paraphrasing upstream strings (DR-5's verbatim rule, activity.ts's docs); translating course data would be a fabrication, not a fix. Kept at minor — both are cosmetic and neither produces a wrong schedule.
### [ux-fail-6] Raw English API/browser error strings are printed verbatim in the Norwegian UI
- Where: `src/lib/planner/data.ts:62` · copy · UNVERIFIED
- Evidence: Observed three distinct leaks in the live DOM. (a) `#26h;-;+ZZZ9999` → course row meta: "fikk ikke hentet detaljer: Not found" (the worker's `{"error":"Not found"}` body, curl-confirmed). (b) /api/course/** stubbed to 500 with `{"error":"Internal error"}` → four rows reading "fikk ikke hentet timeplan: Internal error" / "fikk ikke hentet detaljer: Internal error" (screenshot I1-api-500.png). (c) /api/** aborted → "fikk ikke hentet timeplan: Failed to fetch" ×4 — Chromium's own English exception message. Path: data.ts:57-66 sets `message = body.error` (or the bare status number, or the DOMException message) and pushes `timeplan: ${message}`; plannerApp.ts:954 renders `fikk ikke hentet ${error}` unfiltered. My leak scan matched /Not found/, /Internal/ and /Failed to fetch/ in document.body.innerText.
- Failure: A Norwegian student whose course detail fetch 404s reads "fikk ikke hentet detaljer: Not found" — half Norwegian, half untranslated machine English, and "Not found" tells them nothing about whether the course exists, whether to retry, or whether to blame the site.
- Fix: Stop threading the upstream message into UI copy. In data.ts, classify the failure (`"not-found" | "server" | "network"`) and store that enum on `CourseBundle.errors`; render fixed Norwegian sentences at plannerApp.ts:954 ("fant ikke emnet i katalogen", "NTNU svarte ikke — prøv igjen", "ingen nettforbindelse").
- Verifier: verifier never ran (session limit)
### [ux-fail-8] `hidden` does not hide any `.np-btn`, so "Velg fra studieplanen" is offered on plans that have no study plan
- Where: `src/styles/primitives.css:65` · design-system · UNVERIFIED
- Evidence: On `/planlegger/#26h;-;+BI1006` (no programme at all) the Emner rail renders "Mangler 22,5 sp [Velg fra studieplanen]" above "[Legg til emne]" (screenshot H-B7-BI1006.png). Playwright: `#planner-gap-btn` → `hidden=true display=flex width=160`. Same on `#26h;KNOAND.2024;`. `renderGapLine` does exactly what its own comment asks (plannerApp.ts:645, `elements.gapButton.hidden = pool.length === 0`, with the comment "the rail carried two identical buttons a line apart"), but `.np-btn { display: inline-flex }` at primitives.css:65 is an author rule and therefore beats the UA `[hidden] { display: none }`. site.css:172 already documents and patches this — but only inside `.studieinfo-dialog`. addCourse.ts:121-123 hides `.np-btn`s the same way and is also unpatched.
- Failure: A student with no programme (or one whose study plan is missing) sees a button promising a study-plan picker; clicking it opens the generic whole-catalog add dialog — the same thing the button directly beneath it does. The empty state advertises data the app does not have.
- Fix: One global rule in src/styles/primitives.css beside `.np-btn`: `[hidden] { display: none !important; }` (or `.np-btn[hidden] { display: none; }`), and delete the now-redundant `.studieinfo-dialog [hidden]` special case in site.css:172.
- Verifier: verifier never ran (session limit)
### [week-4] The block's start time is the first thing clipped, because the meta line puts the room name before it
- Where: `src/components/planner/grid.ts:502` · ux · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `metaLine` is `[entry.rooms, entry.startTime].filter(Boolean).join(" · ")` (grid.ts:501-503) and `.planner-block-meta` is `white-space: nowrap; overflow: hidden; text-overflow: ellipsis` (planner-week.css:250-257). Live on both /planlegger/#26h;MTDT.2026; and #26h;BIT.2026;, the TDT4109 block (148 px wide, 72 px tall — under COMPACT_BLOCK_MINUTES, so grid.ts:553 also omits the name and week lines) reports scrollWidth > clientWidth on its meta span; the screenshot shows Friday reading "TDT4109 / Digital undervisn…" with no time at all. Every 2-column split does the same: on /planlegger/#26h;MTIØT.2026; the two 73 px blocks report CLIP on `"S5 · 10:15"` and `"EL5 · 10:15"`. The hour rail marks only whole hours, so nothing else on screen distinguishes 12:15 from 12:00. No adjudicated decision in DESIGN.md §8 fixes the room-before-time order — only a code comment.
- Failure: A student glancing at the week to answer "when is TDT4109 on Friday?" sees "TDT4109 / Digital undervisn…" with the start time cut off, on a block whose top edge sits between the 12:00 and 13:00 rules. The time is only recoverable via the title tooltip (absent on touch) or by opening the popover.
- Fix: grid.ts:502 — swap the order: `return [entry.startTime, entry.rooms].filter(Boolean).join(" · ")`. The ellipsis then eats the room name, which the block's `title`, the aria-label and the popover all carry.
- Verifier: Reproduced the clipping live on three plans and in the rendered screenshot rather than trusting the scrollWidth number alone. Tried to kill it by looking for an adjudicated DESIGN.md §8 decision or PLANNER.md render rule pinning "rom · start" order — there is none, only the grid.ts comment. Downgraded the auditor's "major" to minor: the block's vertical position, the title attribute and the popover all still carry the time, so it is survivable, but the guaranteed second line loses the one fact a student copies into a calendar and the fix is a one-line swap.
### [week-7] Day names are not sticky, so the below-the-fold half of a toggled-on week is five unlabelled columns
- Where: `src/styles/planner-week.css:87` · ux · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: Measured live at 1280×900 on /planlegger/#26h;MTIØT.2026; with "Vis øvinger og labber" on: grid top at y=419 in document coords, grid height 1078 px, document height 1903, viewport 900 — so ~597 px of the week sits below the fold. `.planner-grid-day-header` computes `position: static` (planner-week.css:86-92 sets no sticky) and `.planner-grid-frame` computes `overflow-y: hidden` (line 35-40), so the page itself is the scroller. Scrolled to y=760, the header row's bounding rect is at top=-341 and off screen, leaving the hour rail intact and no day names at all. The BIT screenshot shows the same shape: header row at ~y270, blocks running to 18:00 at ~y1237.
- Failure: Student presses "Vis øvinger og labber" (which grows the week from ~08:00–16:00 to 08:00–20:00), scrolls down to check when an evening lab is, and can no longer tell which weekday column it is in without scrolling back up.
- Fix: planner-week.css — give `.planner-grid-frame` a `max-height: calc(100svh - 10rem)` with `overflow-y: auto` (keeping `overflow-x: auto` for A4's sideways scroll) and make `.planner-grid-day-header` `position: sticky; top: 0; z-index: 2; background: var(--bg)`. Note REVIEW.md U2 deliberately removed an *inert* sticky because the frame was not a vertical scroller — the max-height and the sticky must land together, and the A4 edge-mask behaviour must be re-checked.
- Verifier: Verified the geometry and the computed styles live rather than by reading CSS, including scrolling and re-measuring the header rect. Tried to kill it as contradicting REVIEW.md U2's adjudicated removal of the sticky — U2 removed it because it was inert given a horizontal-only scroller, not because sticky headers were rejected on the merits, so the finding stands, but the fix is more invasive than a one-liner. Held at minor (not major): five evenly spaced columns and blocks that name their course/room/title keep the state survivable.
### [worker-2] 502 bodies pass ntnu-api's raw message (full internal Liferay portlet URL) straight through, and the planner renders it verbatim into a Norwegian course row
- Where: `worker/src/routes.ts:93` · ux · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: Reproduced live: `curl -G --data-urlencode 'version=../../etc' --data-urlencode 'year=2026' http://127.0.0.1:8788/api/course/TDT4100/timetable` → HTTP 502 with body `{"error":"GET https://www.ntnu.no/web/studier/emner?p_p_id=coursedetailsportlet_WAR_courselistportlet&p_p_lifecycle=2&…&_coursedetailsportlet_WAR_courselistportlet_courseCode=TDT4100&year=2026&version=..%2F..%2Fetc -> 500"}`. Traced the render path myself: routes.ts:92-94 `errorJson(err.message, 502)`; message built at `node_modules/ntnu-api/dist/errors.js` / `dist/http.js:161` as `${method} ${finalUrl} …`; `src/lib/planner/data.ts:57-66` copies `body.error` into the thrown Error; data.ts:110 pushes `timeplan: ${message}` into `bundle.errors`; `src/components/planner/plannerApp.ts:953-954` appends `el("span", undefined, "fikk ikke hentet " + error)` into the course row's meta line.
- Failure: NTNU's portlet 500s or times out during a timetable fetch. The planner's TDT4100 row shows a meta line reading `fikk ikke hentet timeplan: GET https://www.ntnu.no/web/studier/emner?p_p_id=…&version=1 -> 500` — unbounded English/URL text in a bokmål, sentence-case UI, inside the flagship surface, in place of a usable message.
- Fix: worker/src/routes.ts:92-94: `console.warn(err.message)` (Workers Logs; observability is already on in wrangler.jsonc) and return a stable token instead of the raw message, e.g. `errorJson("upstream_error", 502)`; map that token to Norwegian copy where `src/lib/planner/data.ts:110/117` build `bundle.errors`.
- Verifier: I tried hard to kill this and knocked most of it down rather than all of it. The auditor's `security` framing does not survive: `src/components/planner/dom.ts:11-13` sets `node.textContent`, so the reflected `?version=` text cannot become markup (no XSS), and the leaked URL is a *public* ntnu.no endpoint that any user of the site could discover — so this is not information disclosure of consequence. I also checked reachability: valid codes 404/200 cleanly (`/api/course/ZZZ9999` → 404 `{"error":"Not found"}`, `?version=99` → 200), so a 502 needs a genuine upstream failure — this is a degraded-state-only defect, not something a normal user hits. What survives is the copy-rule violation in the flagship surface, which `docs/SPEC.md`'s "other NTNUAPIError→502 {error}" line does not waive (it says nothing about which message). Downgraded major → minor and recategorised security → ux.
### [worker-3] A `null` upstream result — which also covers "NTNU answered 200 with an empty body" — is negative-cached for the full positive TTL (24 h plans, 6 h details)
- Where: `worker/src/routes.ts:205` · correctness · PLAUSIBLE
- Evidence: Code path confirmed by reading: routes.ts:205 `await deps.cache.set(key, plan ?? MISSING, PLAN_CACHE_TTL_MS)` uses the same 24 h constant as a success (cache.ts:67); routes.ts:124 does the same with 6 h for details; routes.ts:199/118 turn a `MISSING` hit into `404 {"error":"Not found"}` without re-consulting upstream. That `null` is genuinely overloaded: `node_modules/ntnu-api/dist/http.js:149-151` returns `null` whenever `response.status === 204 || text.trim() === ""`, and `dist/programs.js:250-252` maps that straight to `studyPlan → null`. With `env.CACHE` bound, `TieredCache.set` persists the sentinel to KV with `expirationTtl: 86400` (cache.ts:121-128) and cache.ts:110-114 re-stamps a KV hit into memory with a fresh timestamp.
- Failure: NTNU's Liferay study-plan resource answers one empty 200 while a visitor requests MTDT 2026. Every later request for MTDT/2026 served by that isolate gets `404 {"error":"Not found"}` for up to 24 h with no upstream re-check. Users are not shown an error: `findProgramPlanUncached` (src/components/planner/programPlan.ts:154-165) steps the year back on 404, so they are silently prefilled from the 2025 cohort's plan and see 2025's kull chips.
- Fix: In worker/src/routes.ts give misses their own short TTL: store the sentinel under a separate key (`JSON.stringify(["plan-miss", parsedCode, year])`) with `const MISS_TTL_MS = 10 * 60_000`, read after the real key misses. Same in `handleCourseDetails`. This preserves the "no refetch storm for a genuinely unknown code" property the existing tests assert while capping the blast radius of a transient blank response.
- Verifier: I could not refute the code path — it is exactly as described, and `docs/SPEC.md` only says "Cache null details/plan as sentinel 'missing'" without blessing a TTL, so it is not a waived decision. But I knocked the severity down hard on two grounds the auditor overstated. (1) The trigger is weakly evidenced: the "NTNU answers an empty 200 when it dislikes the parameters" comment the auditor cites (`dist/courses.js:66-68`) is about the *search* endpoint with bad params, not about transient Liferay blips — a real outage far more likely yields 5xx/timeout, which `mapUpstreamError` handles and does *not* cache. I found no evidence of a blank-200 occurring in practice. (2) The "global for 24 h" claim depends on KV, and `wrangler.jsonc` has `kv_namespaces` commented out with `docs/SPEC.md:43` listing "KV namespace creation" as not yet wired — today the blast radius is one short-lived isolate. I also corrected the auditor's downstream claim: `{kind:"error"}` does not "render nothing"; studieinfo.ts:361-368 renders an explicit hint. major → minor, CONFIRMED → PLAUSIBLE.
## POLISH (21)
### [a11y-7] Studieinfo's initial focus lands on the semester select, below the kull chips, so forward Tab covers only Semester → Lagre → Avbryt before wrapping
- Where: `src/components/planner/studieinfo.ts:636` · a11y · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: Reproduced live with a BIT/2025 profile. `focusInitial()` (636-639) focuses `semesterSelect` whenever a programme is staged. Live DOM order of visible focusables in the dialog: `studieinfo-chip-remove` → `Kull 2026` → `Kull 2025` → `Kull 2024` → `#studieinfo-semester-select` → `#studieinfo-save` → `#studieinfo-cancel`; initial focus is the fifth of seven. Forward Tab from there gives T0=Lagre, T1=Avbryt, T2=(body, wrap), T3="Fjern studieprogram". `commit()` (587) refuses to save a staged programme without a kull.
- Failure: A returning student opens Studieinfo to switch kull. Tabbing forward they meet Semester, Lagre and Avbryt before the tab order wraps, so the kull chips are the last thing reached rather than the next — reading order and focus order disagree.
- Fix: In `focusInitial()` focus the first control instead: `chipHost.querySelector<HTMLButtonElement>(".studieinfo-chip-remove")?.focus() ?? semesterSelect.focus()` when a programme is staged, so forward Tab runs programme → kull → retning → semester → Lagre in reading order.
- Verifier: Downgraded hard, from the auditor's `minor` to `polish`, after two refutation attempts that mostly succeeded. (a) The auditor's failure scenario — the student "reasonably concludes kull cannot be changed here" — does not hold: the modal's tab order wraps, so three more Tabs reach the chips, and the chips are visibly rendered above the select with correct `aria-pressed` state. (b) I checked for a documented decision behind focusing the semester (the recent "semester in modal" work, commits f86105b/93c24c5) — none found in docs or comments, so it is not adjudicated. What survives is only that focus order disagrees with reading order; it is a nicety, not a WCAG 2.4.3 failure.
### [app-5] loadBundles() has no fetch-generation token, so a late-resolving bundle for the previous semester's year can overwrite the correct one and is then never refetched
- Where: `src/components/planner/plannerApp.ts:1477` · correctness · PLAUSIBLE
- Evidence: loadBundles (plannerApp.ts:1462-1487) captures `year` at entry; its continuation only checks that the course still exists — `const current = courseStates.get(...); if (!current) return; current.bundle = bundle; current.loading = false;` (1476-1480) — never that plan.semesterId is still the one it fetched for. syncCourseStates (837-848) nulls `state.bundle` and `state.loading` on a semester switch, and data.ts:85-92 memoises per `code:year:version`, so the 2026 and 2027 promises are distinct objects resolving in network order. Once the stale write lands, `bundle !== null` makes the state invisible to the toLoad filter at 1466, so nothing refetches it for the rest of the page load. The neighbouring loadPeriodCourses has exactly this guard (studyPlanFetchToken, 1531/1533). I could NOT produce a user-visible wrong render today: data/semesters.json has timetablePublished true only for 26h, and renderGridAndExams' `showFallback` (plannerApp.ts:1238,1256) replaces the grid with the pre-publish card for 27v/27h, while renderProvenance (1391) prints "Timeplan ikke publisert for …" ahead of the "hentet direkte fra NTNU nå" branch. So the defect is real in code but latent on screen.
- Failure: Once a second semester's timetable is published (27h, same week numbers 34-48 as 26h), a student who switches semester within the first second of load — before the first fetch settles — can get 2026 rooms and times rendered under a "Høst 2027" banner, with provenance claiming "Timeplan hentet direkte fra NTNU nå" and no refetch short of a reload.
- Fix: Give loadBundles the same token loadPeriodCourses already has: capture `const forSemester = plan.semesterId;` at plannerApp.ts:1463 and add `if (forSemester !== plan.semesterId) return;` beside the existing `if (!current) return;` at 1477, plus the same guard before the trailing renders at 1483-1486.
- Verifier: Attacked it as unreachable and mostly succeeded: I traced showFallback/renderProvenance and confirmed no wrong pixels are producible today (only 26h has timetablePublished true), and for 27v the stale 2026 entries would be filtered out by teaching weeks anyway. What survives is the code defect itself — the missing guard, verified by reading loadBundles and confirming the toLoad filter makes the poisoning permanent — plus the asymmetry with loadPeriodCourses right next door. Severity downgraded from minor to polish because no user can see it in the current data.
### [astro-6] /emner/ reads ?q= but never writes it back, so an address bar that arrived with a query keeps advertising the old one
- Where: `src/pages/emner/index.astro:495` · ux · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `src/pages/emner/index.astro:495-497` reads `?q=` once into the input on mount; no `pushState`/`replaceState` anywhere in the file, and the `input` handler (474-484) only calls `render()`. Live: loaded /emner/?q=objektorientert (status "4 treff", input prefilled), retyped "tma4100" → status "1 treff" but `page.url()` still `http://127.0.0.1:8788/emner/?q=objektorientert`. PRODUCT.md:214 lists `?q=` as the page-local view param.
- Failure: Someone who arrived on /emner/?q=objektorientert refines the search to "tma4100", copies the address bar into a chat saying "this one", and the recipient lands on the four objektorientert results. Reloading the page likewise reinstates the old query.
- Fix: In the `input` listener in `src/pages/emner/index.astro`, after `render()`, debounced: `history.replaceState(null, "", value ? `?q=${encodeURIComponent(value)}` : location.pathname)` — `replaceState` so Back still leaves the page in one press.
- Verifier: Reproduced live. Downgraded minor → polish after attacking reachability: nothing in the app produces a `/emner/?q=` link any more (the homepage `?q=` and the 404 prefill form were both deleted in the 2026-07-25 rework Task 13), so the *stale* state requires arriving on a hand-made or previously-shared ?q= URL. The general "my search isn't in the URL" case is a missing enhancement, not a defect, and no doc mandates writeback.
### [astro-7] A lowercase course URL 404s even though the course page exists — /emne/tdt4100/ is 404 while /emne/TDT4100/ is 200
- Where: `worker/src/server.ts:47` · ux · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: curl against the live server: `/emne/tdt4100/` → 404, `/emne/TDT4100/` → 200, `/emne/TDT4100` → 307 to the slash form. `getStaticPaths` (src/pages/emne/[code].astro:9-14) emits only the catalog's exact casing, and `worker/src/server.ts:46-48` passes every non-`/api/` path straight to `env.ASSETS.fetch` with no normalisation. The delivered 404 body does carry the persistent nav ("Planlegger", "Emner") and the footer "Søk i emner", so recovery exists — it just costs the student the code they already had in the URL.
- Failure: A link written lowercase (or a hand-typed URL) — ntnu.example/emne/tdt4100/ — yields "Vi fant ikke siden du lette etter" for a course that exists one casing change away; the visitor has to notice the "Emner" nav item and retype the code.
- Fix: In `worker/src/server.ts`'s non-/api branch (line 46-48): if `ASSETS.fetch` returns 404 for a `/emne/<seg>/` path whose decoded, uppercased form differs from the requested one, 301 to the uppercased path. (I re-verified the auditor's claim that the 5470 codes have no case-insensitive collisions before endorsing this.) Do NOT re-add a search form to 404.astro — see refuted astro-7b.
- Verifier: The status codes are reproduced by curl. I gutted the finding the auditor filed: its headline claim ("404 has no route back into search") is false — the 2026-07-25 corrected nav puts "Emner" in the topbar of every page including 404, and the footer carries "Søk i emner"; I dumped the anchors of the real 404 body to confirm. What survives is only the case-sensitivity, which is genuine but rare enough to be polish, and whose fix lives in the worker, not in 404.astro.
### [conf-4] findConflicts' dedupe key is the overlap window, not the entries, so it drops genuinely clashing entries from its own output (latent — MAX_COLUMNS === 2 hides it today)
- Where: `src/lib/planner/conflicts.ts:65` · correctness · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: Line 65: `const key = ${codeX}|${codeY}|${a.dayNumber}|${start}|${end}|${weeks.join(",")}` — built from the intersection, not from the two entries. Two conflicts from different entry pairs that produce the same intersection collapse, and the second pair's entries never reach the returned Conflict[]. Real geometry from the live API: TDT4109's only 26h lecture is "Digital forelesning første og siste uker i semesteret" Fri 12:15–13:00 weeks 34-35,45-46; SYG1000 has Friday "Forelesning" at 08:15–15:00, 12:15–14:00 and 11:15–13:00 (weeks 33-38,40-47). All three intersect TDT4109 at exactly 12:15–13:00 over weeks {34,35,45,46}, so one conflict is recorded and two are dropped. grid.ts consumes the dropped set for `groupsByEntry` (line 788), `clashWindowFor`, `blockAriaLabel`'s "kolliderer med" and the note's flash targets.
- Failure: No user-visible failure today. It becomes visible the moment layout.ts:42's `MAX_COLUMNS` is raised to 3: the SYG1000 Friday 12:15–14:00 lecture would render in its own column with no `is-clash` class, no clash band, and an aria-label omitting "kolliderer med TDT4109" — a block that really does collide, drawn clean.
- Fix: conflicts.ts line 65: key `seen` on the entries' own spans instead of the intersection — `${codeX}|${codeY}|${a.dayNumber}|${aStart}-${aEnd}|${bStart}-${bEnd}|${weeks.join(",")}`. That still collapses the identical-parallel-group case the comment at lines 37-39 exists for (identical rows have identical spans) without eating distinct slots. Add a regression test with the SYG1000/TDT4109 geometry.
- Verifier: I tried hard to make this bite and could not, and I proved why it cannot rather than just failing to find a case: for any dropped pair, the dropped entry D, the kept entry K and the partner P all contain the identical overlap window W, so all three mutually overlap; `dayClusters` therefore puts them in one cluster and `layoutDay` computes rawCols >= 3 > MAX_COLUMNS === 2, collapsing it into a pile whose `clashWindowFor(cluster)` re-flags it from K's surviving group. I also checked the escape hatch — `isBandEntry` (grid.ts:291) requires `!entry.isLecture`, so no lecture is ever exempted from packing. Downstream counting is unaffected too: `groupConflicts` keys on day|start|end, so dropped duplicates would have merged into the same group and `conflictCount` is unchanged; planClash keys partners on `other|day|start` and is likewise immune. Kept only at polish, with severity downgraded from the auditor's minor: the contract violation is real and the fix is one line, but no user reaches it in the shipped configuration.
### [conf-7] analyzeExams is dead code that duplicates examSchedule.ts with a different "tett" rule, kept alive by 34 lines of green tests
- Where: `src/lib/planner/conflicts.ts:198` · correctness · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `grep -rn 'analyzeExams|ExamRow\b|ExamInput' src/ e2e/ tests/ worker/` returns hits only inside conflicts.ts itself (lines 172-231) and tests/planner/conflicts.test.ts (10 cases from line 197). The live model is examSchedule.ts, whose header says date math is done "entirely with Date.UTC day-differencing over hand-parsed 'YYYY-MM-DD' ISO strings … (no Date.parse)" while conflicts.ts:218 uses `Date.parse` on the same strings. They also disagree on the spacing rule: conflicts.ts:224 sets `tight` only when `gapDays === 1`; examSchedule.ts:97 sets `current.tight = gap <= 2`. The file whose header advertises itself as "the conflict engine … and exam-date collision/gap analysis" therefore ships a second, unused, subtly divergent exam analyser.
- Failure: No user-facing failure — nothing calls it. The failure is maintenance: a future change to the exam-spacing rule made in conflicts.ts (the file that advertises exam analysis in its header) is invisible on the page, and `npm test` stays green because the tests exercise the dead copy.
- Fix: Delete `ExamInput`, `ExamRow`, `MS_PER_DAY` and `analyzeExams` (conflicts.ts lines 172-231) plus the `describe("analyzeExams")` block in tests/planner/conflicts.test.ts, and drop the exam clause from the module header at line 2-3 so it describes only timetable clash detection.
- Verifier: Verified the grep myself across src/, e2e/, tests/ and worker/ — the only production exam path is examList.ts → examSchedule.ts, and it never imports conflicts.ts. I discarded the auditor's `Date.parse` timezone argument as an actual bug: ES5+ specifies date-only ISO strings as UTC, so `Date.parse("2026-12-21")` is deterministic and the day-differencing is correct; the real defect is the duplication plus the divergent `tight` threshold. Kept at the auditor's polish severity.
### [crawler-8] transform.mjs's contract docstring states a version count that contradicts the file it describes (220 vs 293)
- Where: `crawler/transform.mjs:117` · data · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: transform.mjs:117-119 documents `toSearchIndex`'s output as "`version` (index 4) is what DR-4 needs to fetch the right timetable — 220 courses are not version \"1\"". Measured over the function's actual output, `public/data/search-index.json`: 5 470 rows, version histogram `{"1":5177,"A":228,"2":50,"3":10,"B":3,"C":2}` → 293 non-"1" rows, which is what docs/SPEC.md:292 states ("293 of 5 470 rows are not `1`"). I also computed the restricted population: rows that are non-"1" and offered in the canonical year = exactly 220 — i.e. the docstring's number is the pre-two-year-union figure that survived the C1 rework in the file that is the contract's source of truth.
- Failure: A maintainer reading transform.mjs to reason about DR-4 version coverage undercounts re-versioned courses by 25% and has two mutually contradicting numbers in the repo (SPEC says 293, the code says 220) with no way to tell which is stale without recomputing.
- Fix: crawler/transform.mjs:117 — state both populations instead of one stale number: "293 of 5 470 index rows are not version \"1\" (220 of them offered in the canonical year)". crawler-5's artifact test is what would keep it honest.
- Verifier: I looked for a reading under which 220 is correct for what the sentence describes — it is the count for canonical-year rows only, which I computed to be exactly 220, but the sentence describes `toSearchIndex`'s output, all 5 470 rows of it, where the answer is 293. So the number is genuinely stale rather than a different-but-valid measure. Kept at polish: comment-only, no user impact.
### [ds-4] `.np-ruled--hours` is a documented primitive with zero callers — the exact system debt DESIGN §5 deleted `.np-kbd`/`.np-tile`/`.np-lift` for
- Where: `src/styles/primitives.css:333` · design-system · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `grep -rn 'np-ruled--hours' src/ e2e/ worker/` returns three hits, none of them a caller: src/components/planner/grid.ts:298 (inside a comment), src/styles/tokens.css:83 (inside a comment), and src/styles/primitives.css:333 (the definition itself). In the built output the string appears only in `dist/_astro/Layout.6o6mCF-a.css` — no dist HTML or JS contains it — so the rule ships in the sitewide bundle to every visitor and matches nothing. DESIGN §5 states the policy verbatim: "Deleted — do not reach for these, they no longer exist: `.np-kbd` (zero users), `.np-tile` (zero users), `.np-lift` (zero users). A documented primitive with no caller is system debt." Meanwhile planner-week.css:107-112 hand-rolls one of the primitive's three gradients (`linear-gradient(var(--ruling-hour) 1px, transparent 1px); background-size: 100% calc(var(--cell) * 4)`).
- Failure: Contributor-facing only. A contributor reading DESIGN §4/§5 or PLANNER §2 adds `.np-ruled--hours` to a new planning surface and gets a 15-minute square field plus an hour rule anchored to the content box — a different origin from the live week's rule, which planner-week.css:99-105 documents as the source of a ~22 px misregistration. Two implementations of one line, one of them untested and unreachable.
- Fix: Delete `.np-ruled--hours` (src/styles/primitives.css:329-342) and strike it from DESIGN.md §4/§5 and PLANNER.md:52-53, the same way `.np-kbd`/`.np-tile`/`.np-lift` were struck; keep the hand-rolled hour rule in planner-week.css:107-112, which is the one anchored correctly. Fold this into whichever direction ds-1's doc amendment takes.
- Verifier: Tried to refute by finding any runtime caller — checked src, e2e, worker and the built dist for the literal string; the only three hits are two comments and the definition. Also checked whether `.np-ruled` itself is dead (it is not: the homepage frame uses it), so the finding is correctly scoped to the `--hours` modifier alone. Downgraded from the auditor's minor to polish: there is no user-visible effect and the cost is a few hundred bytes plus a stale doc reference. Kept rather than refuted only because DESIGN §5 names this exact situation as a rule violation with a named remedy, so it is measurable against a binding doc rather than taste.
### [ds-6] Data-Is-Mono: the sitewide studieinfo chip sets programme code + kull + semester in the grotesk, ~110 px above the same three facts in mono
- Where: `src/layouts/Layout.astro:156` · design-system · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: Live on /planlegger/ with an MTDT/2025 profile: `.studieinfo-chip` computes {text:"MTDT · 2025 · Høst 2026", fontFamily:'"Schibsted Grotesk", system-ui, …', fontSize:"13.44px"}. The text is written by Layout.astro:224 (`${plan.program.code} · ${plan.program.cohort} · ${semesterLabel(...)}`) into the element declared at Layout.astro:155-159; neither `.np-btn` (primitives.css:65-80) nor `.studieinfo-chip` (site.css:95-100) declares a font-family, so it inherits `--font-sans`. Directly below, the same three facts render in Spline Sans Mono: `.planner-title-code` "MTDT" (plannerApp.ts:502) and `el("span", "np-data", 'kull ' + cohort)` / `el("span", "np-data", semesterLabel(...))` (plannerApp.ts:517, 519). DESIGN §3 Data-Is-Mono: "every time, date, week number, course code, credit count, kicker and label" is mono; the chip's filled state contains no verb at all.
- Failure: On /planlegger/ the strings "MTDT · 2025 · Høst 2026" (topbar, grotesk) and "MTDT" + "kull 2025 · Høst 2026" (h1 and context line, mono) sit within roughly 110 px of each other in two typefaces, so one set of facts reads as two kinds of information.
- Fix: Layout.astro:224 — when a programme is set, build the label from `.np-data` spans (or add `.np-data` to the chip in Layout.astro:156) so the filled state carries the data voice; keep the empty state "Velg studieprogram" in the grotesk, since that one really is a verb phrase.
- Verifier: Tried to refute on the grounds that `.np-btn` is specified as grotesk ("grotesk 500 label, sentence verbs", DESIGN §5), so mono would fight the primitive. That defence only covers the *empty* state — the filled state is three data fragments joined by middots with no verb, which is precisely `.np-data`'s remit, and §5 anticipates the mono case with `.np-toggle` ("selectable mono tag"). Also checked the chip is not somewhere users never see both voices at once: it is in the sitewide topbar, so on /planlegger/ both renderings are on screen simultaneously — measured the DOM positions implicitly via the full-page screenshot. Kept at polish, agreeing with the auditor: it is a voice inconsistency with zero functional cost.
### [edit-7] HTML entities from the upstream timetable render literally in group labels and grid blocks ("MTELSYS &#38; MTTK")
- Where: `src/lib/planner/groups.ts:61` · copy · UNVERIFIED
- Evidence: p7.mjs's popover dump for TMA4400 contains the option label 'Forelesning 1 MTELSYS &#38; MTTK', rendered exactly that way in the picker (screenshot Y-after-lecture-pick.png, third radio). Confirmed at the source: `curl -s 'http://127.0.0.1:8788/api/course/TMA4400/timetable?year=2026' | grep -o '&#[0-9]*;'` returns `&#38;`, so the upstream `title` field is HTML-encoded and `rawGroupName` (groups.ts:60-62) passes it straight into `el("span", …, text)` as a text node.
- Failure: An MTELSYS/MTTK student opening the TMA4400 group picker (or reading the block label in the week, which uses the same `title`) sees "Forelesning 1 MTELSYS &#38; MTTK" — machine escaping surfacing in Norwegian UI copy that the design system says is sentence-case prose.
- Fix: Decode numeric/named HTML entities once where the title is normalised — in `rawGroupName` (groups.ts:60-62) and grid.ts's block-label path, or better once in `data.ts`'s timetable mapping so every consumer gets clean text. A three-entry replace (`&#38;`/`&amp;`, `&#39;`, `&quot;`) covers what the crawl actually contains; keep the upstream-shape knowledge in ntnu-api if it belongs there.
- Verifier: verifier never ran (session limit)
### [exams-7] Exams already sat are indistinguishable from upcoming ones and keep their red "tett" clash ink
- Where: `src/lib/planner/examSchedule.ts:97` · ux · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `ExamListRow` carries no past/future notion: `daysFromToday` is set on the first upcoming row only (examSchedule.ts:104-109) and every other row renders identically through `examRow` (examList.ts:104-112), while `tight` is computed over every adjacent pair regardless of date (examSchedule.ts:97). The planner stylesheet has no past-state rule — `:global(.exam-row)` at src/pages/planlegger/index.astro:384 and `:global(.exam-gap.is-tight) { color: var(--clash); }` at :415 are the only row/connector rules. Observed in the Oslo clock probe at in-page `Fri Dec 11 2026 00:30 +0100`: `on 9. des TDT4120` → `1 dag mellomrom · tett` (clash ink) → `to 10. des TDT4195` → `samme dag` → `to 10. des TFY4205` — a red spacing warning about two exams both already over.
- Failure: During the January exam window (26h stays current until the nightly crawl flips it) a student with five exams sees the three already sat at the top of the list, styled exactly like the two remaining, with clash-coloured "tett" connectors warning about spacing that is now history. Once exams-2 is fixed and the last exam has passed, nothing marks where "now" is at all — no row carries a countdown.
- Fix: Add `past: boolean` (`date < todayIso`) to `ExamListRow` in src/lib/planner/examSchedule.ts, suppress the `is-tight` class in `examGap` (src/components/planner/examList.ts:128-137) when both rows of a connector are past, and give `.exam-row[data-past]` a muted treatment in src/pages/planlegger/index.astro. No new copy needed.
- Verifier: Reproduced the red past-vs-past "tett" connector under an installed Oslo clock. Tried hard to kill it as taste: grepped docs/PLANNER.md and the examSchedule/examList headers for any deliberate "the list shows the whole semester including past sittings" decision — none — and checked the brief's deferred-polish list, which does not cover it. It stays at `polish` because nothing shown is false (the dates and gaps are correct, just stale) and the fix is cosmetic. Not worth a fix budget before exams-1.
### [grid-7] grid.ts documents a "+N til" overflow chip that no longer exists, including a doc comment that calls the still-load-bearing `dayClusters` vestigial
- Where: `src/components/planner/grid.ts:410` · correctness · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: grep for "+N til" in src/components/planner/grid.ts finds five comment sites and no code: :87 and :92 ("What a clicked block (or \"+N til\" chip) hands its listener" / "joined codes for a \"+N til\" chip"), :121 (`onBlockClick` "Called when a block or a \"+N til\" chip is clicked"), :410-411 ("the renderer needs the cluster only to place one \"+N til\" chip over the pile's overflow") and :837 ("an overflow entry stands behind the \"+N til\" chip"). The chip was replaced by the pile (layout.ts:14-22, grid.ts:566-576). :410-411 is not merely stale but inverted: `dayClusters` now drives the entire pile branch (grid.ts:877-895), the opposite of "only to place one chip". `pileDetail`'s parameter is still named `hidden` (:629) though nothing is hidden.
- Failure: A maintainer reading `dayClusters`' doc at grid.ts:404-412 is told the cluster computation exists only to place an overflow chip that grep proves does not exist, concludes it is dead code, and deletes it — breaking every pile, since grid.ts:877-895 branches on it.
- Fix: Rewrite the five comment sites (grid.ts:87, 92, 121, 410, 837) to describe the pile and rename `pileDetail(hidden)` to `pileDetail(entries)`; the module header at :24-27 already has correct wording. The same staleness exists in docs/SPEC.md:141-142 ("MAX_COLUMNS = 3", "+N til"), docs/PRODUCT.md:52 and docs/ROADMAP.md:34-35 — a docs-dimension item, but the code comments are the ones a maintainer acts on.
- Verifier: Verified every cited line by reading the file and by grepping for "+N til" across src/ (zero code hits, comments only). Tried to kill it as harmless commentary; :410-411 survives because it makes an affirmatively false claim about what the cluster is for and points at load-bearing code that reads as deletable. Severity polish — nothing a user sees.
### [groups-8] HTML entities in upstream group titles render literally in the picker and on blocks
- Where: `src/lib/planner/groups.ts:60` · copy · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `curl /api/course/TMA4400/timetable?year=2026` returns the title `"Forelesning 1 MTELSYS &#38; MTTK"` verbatim. Live DOM dump of the TMA4400 popover on /planlegger/#26h;MTDT.2026; renders the radio label exactly as "Forelesning 1 MTELSYS &#38; MTTK". rawGroupName (groups.ts:60-62) passes `title` through untouched and popover.ts:216 sets it via textContent, so the numeric entity is displayed rather than "&". The same string reaches the block's visible name, `title` attribute and `aria-label` via grid.ts:227/496.
- Failure: An MTELSYS/MTTK student choosing between TMA4400's lecture clusters reads "Forelesning 1 MTELSYS &#38; MTTK" and has to guess whether the typo is in the course data or in this site; a screen-reader user hears the entity spelled out.
- Fix: Decode numeric/named HTML entities once at the fetch boundary — a small `decodeEntities` over `title`/`name` in fetchCourseBundle's timetable mapping (src/lib/planner/data.ts) — so keys, labels, block text and aria-labels all agree. Decoding inside rawGroupName alone would leave grid.ts's own label untouched.
- Verifier: Verified the entity is in the upstream payload (curl) and not introduced by our code, and that it survives to the rendered DOM (live label dump). Considered refuting it as upstream's problem, not ours — but we already normalise these strings into slugs and we own every surface they render on, and it is one affected course out of the ~45 I sampled. Kept at polish, which is where the auditor put it.
### [mob-6] The block popover's group picker uses 20.8px rows with 13x13px native inputs, bypassing the --control-h token every other control on the page honours
- Where: `src/styles/site.css:504` · design-system · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: Reproduced at 390x844 and 360x740 with `pointer: coarse` true and `--control-h` = 44px. Tapping TMA4412's block opens the popover as a bottom sheet (site.css:457-468; measured box 0,513.2 -> 390x844). Its five group rows each measure 356 x 20.8 with a contained `<input>` of 13 x 13, at y = 637.9 / 662.7 / 687.5 / 712.3 / 737.2 — a 24.8px pitch — for "Forelesning", "Regneverksted", "Mattelab 1", "Mattelab 2", "Øvingsforelesing". `.planner-popover-group-row` (site.css:504-510) sets only `display: flex`, `align-items: center`, a gap and a font size; `.planner-popover-group-row input` (site.css:512-514) sets only `accent-color`. Every other control measured on the page is 44px tall.
- Failure: A student picking a lab parallel from the bottom sheet has "Mattelab 1" and "Mattelab 2" 24.8px apart centre-to-centre. A mis-tap silently changes which parallel is drawn, and the popover mirrors selection to the store on change, so the wrong group is persisted into the plan and the share hash.
- Fix: In src/styles/site.css:504 add `min-height: var(--control-h)` to `.planner-popover-group-row` and raise `.planner-popover-group-list`'s gap (site.css:498-502) above `--space-1`; scale the native input to `width: 1.15rem; height: 1.15rem` in the rule at :512. `--control-h` already carries 44px on touch, so no media query is needed.
- Verifier: Tried hard to kill this one and mostly succeeded on the framing. The auditor filed it as an a11y failure; it is not one under WCAG 2.2 SC 2.5.8, because the 24.8px pitch clears the 24px spacing exception (adjacent 24px-diameter target circles do not overlap) and the tappable row is 356px wide. I also read the rendered bottom sheet back (v-pop-390.png) and the picker looks comfortable, not cramped. What survives is narrower and real: the geometry is as measured, and these rows are the only interactive controls on the page that ignore `--control-h` — a design-system inconsistency on the control PRODUCT names as the one editing toggle. Downgraded minor -> polish and recategorised a11y -> design-system.
### [perf-4] Every page statically imports the whole 11.9 KB studieinfo dialog module to read one string constant
- Where: `src/layouts/Layout.astro:176` · perf · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `Layout.astro:176` imports `OPEN_STUDIEINFO_EVENT` (defined at `src/components/planner/studieinfo.ts:54` as the literal `"np:open-studieinfo"`) and uses it once, at line 212, inside a `location.pathname.startsWith("/planlegger/")` branch. The built Layout entry starts `import{O as s}from"./studieinfo.D5p2DPur.js"` — not tree-shakeable. `dist/_astro/studieinfo.D5p2DPur.js` is 11 941 B raw / **4 422 B gzip** and contains the whole dialog. My Playwright waterfall confirms it is actually fetched on `/` and `/emner/` as well as `/planlegger/`. Home-page JS totals ≈16.6 KB gzip across 9 chunks, so this dead module is ~27 % of it. Same shape at `src/pages/index.astro:105`, which imports all of `data/semesters.json` (built to `semesters.Ct55FGL3.js`, 12 013 B raw / 1 734 B gzip, 24 semesters with unused `nameEn`/`nameNn`) just to read `semestersData.current?.id`.
- Failure: Student lands on `/` from a shared link on 4G: the browser fetches, parses and evaluates 4.4 KB gzip of programme-dialog code the page can never execute, and (see perf-2) revalidates it again on `/emner/` and on every course page.
- Fix: Move the constant to a leaf module, e.g. `src/components/planner/studieinfoEvent.ts` exporting `OPEN_STUDIEINFO_EVENT`, imported by both `Layout.astro:176` and `src/components/planner/studieinfo.ts:54`.
- Verifier: Verified the import survives into the bundle (read the built chunk's first import statement) and that the browser really requests it on `/` and `/emner/`, not only on the planner — so it is not dead-code speculation. Corrected the auditor's framing: the honest cost is 4 422 B gzip, not 11.9 KB, which is why I downgraded minor → polish. Kept only because the fix is a two-line refactor with zero behaviour risk.
### [perf-5] `/api/course/:code` 404s are `no-store` even when the worker is serving its own 6-hour cached MISSING sentinel
- Where: `worker/src/routes.ts:49` · perf · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `errorJson` (routes.ts:40-53) hard-codes `"Cache-Control": "no-store"`, and `handleCourseDetails` (lines 116-124) returns `errorJson("Not found", 404)` on both a fresh upstream miss and a **cache hit** of the `MISSING` sentinel it stored for `DETAILS_CACHE_TTL_MS`; `handleProgramPlan` (lines 190-198) does the same. Live: `/api/course/TMA4100` → `HTTP/1.1 404` + `Cache-Control: no-store`, vs `/api/course/TDT4100` → `public, max-age=300, s-maxage=21600`. Reachable: `src/pages/emne/[code].astro:436` calls `mountCourseDetails(code)` unconditionally and `src/components/site/courseDetails.ts:193` fetches `/api/course/:code` with no `isOffered` guard, so every visit to a not-currently-taught course page hits it. I re-counted the population from the shipped `public/data/search-index.json`: 703 of 5 470 rows have `offeredYears` excluding the index year 2026.
- Failure: A student with TMA4100 in their plan reloads /planlegger/ or revisits /emne/TMA4100/. Every other course answers from the browser cache with transferSize 0; TMA4100's details call goes to the network every single time, and `no-store` also stops Cloudflare's edge from holding an answer the worker itself considers stable for six hours.
- Fix: Give `errorJson` an optional `ttlMs` and pass `DETAILS_CACHE_TTL_MS` / `PLAN_CACHE_TTL_MS` on the two `MISSING`-sentinel 404 paths only (routes.ts ~line 117 and ~line 197), emitting `public, max-age=300, s-maxage=21600`. Keep `no-store` for 400/429/502, which genuinely must not be cached.
- Verifier: Verified the header live, verified the code path really is the cached-sentinel branch and not only a cold miss, verified the client has no guard that would skip the call for non-offered courses, and re-counted the 703 figure myself rather than trusting it. Then attacked the severity: I timed the 404 at 2.2–2.8 ms from a warm isolate and the body is 21 bytes, so the cost to a user is one RTT per page load, not a broken state — downgraded minor → polish.
### [perf-6] 4 043 B of identical page-scoped CSS is inlined into all 5 470 course pages instead of being one cacheable stylesheet
- Where: `astro.config.mjs:4` · perf · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: `astro.config.mjs` has no `build` block, so Astro's default `inlineStylesheets: "auto"` applies. Measured myself: `dist/emne/TDT4100/index.html` is 12 329 B with exactly one `<style>` block of 4 043 B; `dist/emne/AAR1025/index.html` is 12 345 B with a byte-identical block (string comparison returned true). 5 470 course pages × 4 043 B = 22.1 MB of the 68 985 165 B `dist`. Both other stylesheets are already hashed files (`/_astro/planner-week.BABxxX4K.css`, `/_astro/Layout.6o6mCF-a.css`). Honest per-view cost, which the auditor overstated: the page brotli-compresses to 3 065 B with the block and 2 253 B without — **812 B brotli** of duplicated bytes per course-page view (gzip: 3 629 vs 2 680), and the live server does serve brotli (`Content-Encoding: br`).
- Failure: Student comparing three courses opens /emne/TDT4100/, /emne/TDT4109/ and /emne/TMA4100/ and re-downloads the same ~0.8 KB brotli of identical CSS three times where a hashed stylesheet would have been reused; the nightly crawl redeploy also ships 22 MB of duplicated bytes.
- Fix: Set `build: { inlineStylesheets: "never" }` in `astro.config.mjs` so the course-page CSS becomes a hashed `/_astro/*.css`. Only worth doing together with perf-2's `_headers` — without immutable caching it trades an inline block for one more revalidated request and could regress first-view FCP, so re-measure FCP after.
- Verifier: Re-measured everything rather than trusting the report: byte-identical blocks across two unrelated courses, dist total and page count, and I compressed the page with and without the block to get the real wire cost. That last measurement is what kills the auditor's severity — 812 B brotli per view is not a "minor" user problem, and `inlineStylesheets: "auto"` is a deliberate framework tradeoff rather than a bug. Kept at polish only because the duplication really is 33 % of the HTML and the fix is one config line.
### [plan-8] "Velg fra studieplanen" stays visible with an empty pool because .np-btn's author display beats the UA [hidden] rule
- Where: `src/components/planner/plannerApp.ts:645` · ux · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: renderGapLine sets elements.gapButton.hidden = pool.length === 0 (line 645) and only rewrites the label when the pool is non-empty, so the markup default "Velg fra studieplanen" (planlegger/index.astro:107) stays. .np-btn { display: inline-flex } (src/styles/primitives.css:65-66) is an author rule and therefore wins over the UA [hidden] { display: none } regardless of specificity; the containing .planner-gap-line:not([hidden]) is display: flex, so the button blockifies to flex. Measured in Chromium on three separate profiles with an empty pool — #26h;CMED.2026.CMED26-V-T;, #26h;MPPR.2026;, #26h;BSPL.2025.BSPL25-V-TRONDHEIM; — each reports {hiddenProp: true, hasAttr: true, display: "flex", height: 36, text: "Velg fra studieplanen"}. On #27v;MSCHEM.2025;, where the pool has 1 row, the label correctly reads "Velg fra studieplanen (1)". src/styles/site.css:167-173 documents this exact cascade trap and fixes it, but only inside .studieinfo-dialog.
- Failure: On any programme+kull whose period offers no electives, the rail shows an unlabelled-count "Velg fra studieplanen" button directly above "Legg til emne"; both are wired to the same addCourseDialog.open() (plannerApp.ts:826/829-830), so it promises the study plan and delivers the whole catalogue — precisely the duplicate-button case the comment at plannerApp.ts:639-644 says was removed.
- Fix: Add [hidden] { display: none !important; } to src/styles/base.css (or .np-btn[hidden] { display: none } beside the rule in primitives.css) so every hidden-toggled .np-btn on the page actually hides.
- Verifier: Reproduced in the browser rather than reasoning about the cascade: hidden property true, hidden attribute present, computed display flex, bounding height 36px. Checked for a compensating rule — the only [hidden] overrides in the codebase are .studieinfo-dialog [hidden] (site.css:167-173) and .planner-gap-line:not([hidden]) (planlegger/index.astro:490); neither covers the button. Kept at polish: it is a stray affordance, not wrong data, and it only appears alongside the far worse plan-1/plan-2 emptiness.
### [search-8] The aria-live status is rewritten on every keystroke — one word queues ten result counts at a screen reader
- Where: `src/pages/emner/index.astro:246` · a11y · PLAUSIBLE
- Evidence: `#emner-status` has `aria-live="polite"` (index.astro:23) and `setStatus` (246-250) replaces its textContent synchronously from `render()`, which runs on every `input` with no debounce (474-484). Measured live with a MutationObserver while typing "matematikk" character by character: 10 writes — "viser 200 av 3777 treff", "viser 200 av 1325 treff", "viser 200 av 383 treff", "viser 200 av 238 treff", "132 treff" ×4, "112 treff" ×2. Nine describe prefixes the user never intended to search.
- Failure: A screen-reader user types "matematikk" and gets up to ten polite announcements of counts for prefixes they were never searching, continuing to arrive after they stop typing; the only true one ("112 treff") is last in the queue.
- Fix: Debounce only the live-region write in src/pages/emner/index.astro (the DOM render can stay synchronous — a 200-row rebuild is a couple of milliseconds): keep a `statusTimer`, `clearTimeout` at the top of `render()`, set the text in a ~350 ms `setTimeout`, and leave the empty-query hint immediate.
- Verifier: I reproduced the mechanism exactly (10 writes, listed above) but could not observe the harm: no real AT here, and how aggressively NVDA/JAWS/VoiceOver coalesce rapid polite updates varies — some drop all but the last. So the count is fact, the queued-speech consequence is inference. Kept at polish only, and I would not spend more than the three-line debounce on it.
### [tests-6] grid.ts's dayClusters is an untested second copy of layoutDay's clustering rule
- Where: `src/components/planner/grid.ts:413` · test-coverage · PLAUSIBLE
- Evidence: Diffed the two implementations line by line. src/lib/planner/layout.ts:64-69 sorts by (start, end, id) and flushes on `item.start >= clusterMaxEnd`; src/components/planner/grid.ts:413-438 sorts by (startTime, endTime, blockId) and flushes on `start >= maxEnd` — the same comparator and the same boundary rule, so today the two partitions are provably identical and there is NO live defect. renderGrid (grid.ts:875-895) joins them: slotById comes from layoutDay(layoutInput), the iteration comes from dayClusters(packable), and the pile verdict is read from `slotById.get(blockId(cluster[0]))?.piled`. `grep -rn dayClusters src/ tests/ e2e/` returns exactly two hits, both inside grid.ts — nothing imports or exercises it, and tests/ contains no file for grid.ts (1 015 lines) or plannerApp.ts. The only pile coverage is flows.pw.ts:303-347, whose three stubbed entries are all 08:15-10:00, i.e. exactly simultaneous, so it never exercises a touching (`start === prevEnd`) or chained boundary — the one rule the duplication could diverge on.
- Failure: Not reachable today. It becomes reachable the moment one copy is edited without the other: change grid.ts:427 to `start > maxEnd` (or change layout.ts:64 alone) and an 08:15-10:00 lecture followed by a 10:00-12:00 one forms one cluster in dayClusters but two in layoutDay. cluster[0]'s slot says piled:false, so both entries take the else-branch and look up slots reporting cols:1, col:0 — two full-width blocks stacked on top of each other in one weekday column, one hiding the other. `mise run check` stays green (layout.test.ts still passes, dayClusters is untested) and the pile e2e stays green (its items are simultaneous, unaffected by the boundary rule).
- Fix: src/components/planner/grid.ts:877 — drop dayClusters and group `packable` by `slotById.get(blockId(e))?.cluster`; LayoutSlot already carries a `cluster` index (layout.ts:36) for exactly this, so the partition comes from the single tested source. If the duplicate must stay, export it and add tests/planner/gridClusters.test.ts mirroring layout.test.ts's touching-boundary and chain cases.
- Verifier: Tried hard to kill this one and mostly succeeded. Compared the two algorithms character by character — same sort keys, same `>=` boundary, same input set — so the partitions are identical right now and no user can reach a wrong state; the auditor's failure_scenario is a hypothetical future edit, not concrete state, which is exactly what the brief says does not count. I also checked whether the duplication is undocumented and it is not: grid.ts:404-412 carries a comment stating it deliberately mirrors layoutDay's clustering. What survives is only the narrow, true test-coverage claim — a duplicated invariant with tests on one copy and a stubbed e2e that structurally cannot exercise the boundary rule. Downgraded minor → polish and PLAUSIBLE on that basis; report it as a cheap hardening, not a defect.
### [worker-7] No HTTP method gate on /api/* — POST/PUT/DELETE/OPTIONS are served as GETs with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300` — and `/api` returns the HTML 404 page while `/api/` returns the JSON envelope
- Where: `worker/src/server.ts:44` · correctness · CONFIRMED
- Evidence: Reproduced live against 127.0.0.1:8788: `curl -X POST /api/course/TDT4100` → `200`, 5514 bytes; `-X PUT` → 200/5514; `-X DELETE` → 200/5514; `-X OPTIONS` → 200/5514, no `Allow` and no CORS headers. Confirmed in source that `fetch(request, env)` (server.ts:44-87) never reads `request.method`. Separately `curl -i /api` → `404 text/html; charset=utf-8` (falls through to `env.ASSETS.fetch` because the guard at server.ts:48 is `pathname.startsWith("/api/")`) while `curl -i /api/` → `404 {"error":"Not found"}` as `application/json`.
- Failure: A misdirected non-GET (scanner, browser extension, a client that pre-flights) is answered with a full data payload marked publicly cacheable for 5 minutes and a real upstream fetch behind it, instead of `405 Allow: GET, HEAD`. An API client that hits `/api` by mistake gets an HTML body and a JSON parse error rather than the `{"error":"Not found"}` envelope every other /api path returns.
- Fix: In worker/src/server.ts, right after computing `pathname`: change the guard on line 48 to `if (pathname !== "/api" && !pathname.startsWith("/api/")) return env.ASSETS.fetch(request);`, then before routing return 405 with `Allow: GET, HEAD` and `Cache-Control: no-store` for any method other than GET/HEAD.
- Verifier: Every factual claim reproduced with curl. I tried to kill it on impact and mostly succeeded: no CORS headers are emitted, so cross-origin JS cannot read these responses regardless of method; there is no state to mutate (the API is read-only), so a POST-as-GET is not a security hole; and no real user or the site's own code ever issues a non-GET. `docs/SPEC.md` documents the routing as "route on `url.pathname`; no router framework" but says nothing about methods, so it is not waived — it is just hygiene with no user harm. Downgraded minor → polish; kept only because the fix is three lines and the `/api` vs `/api/` envelope inconsistency is a genuine contract wart.
